Summary

Carol and Zosia Meet before the joining AU

Carol is in Berlin for a writing residency, she becomes friends with a group of her residency mates who introduce her to Zosia, a young ex-pat who lives with more confidence than Carol has even been able to muster.

Notes

Premise inspired by zosiagf's great work: She was Sunshine, I was Midnight Rain

In this Carol hasn't written Wycaro yet, Carol is 33 and Zosia is 23, Since we haven't seen RealZosia I'm basing her off of what we have heard about Raban since she is his twin and there are so many parallels between the two. I also think it will be more exciting when i get into the show content to have such a strong contrast :)

✓♥︎

It's All Coming Back to Me Now

by Moussse3

https://archiveofourown.org/works/76355536

    Chapter 1: Part One: First Meeting

    2005, 1 year before meeting Helen, 20 years before the Joining 

    Carol applied for the residency on a whim, she’d been drunk and an old friend she hadn’t seen or heard from in years emailed her out of the blue with a link to some Berlin based writing program. She’d been shocked when she received her acceptance, mostly because she didn’t remember applying.

    She decided to go because she’d never left the United States and was looking for any reason to quit her current job, where she was filing documents for an accountant who had no concept of dental hygiene or basic manors. The plane ride was horrible, she was seated in the very last row of economy with a crying baby. She got no sleep, despite the Ambien she took to help and arrived jetlagged and drowsy. Finding her newly sublet apartment had been just as bad, half of the buildings weren't marked with a number, and when she tried asking for help she managed to find the only Germans in the country who didn’t speak a word of English. 

    When she finally made it inside, she dropped all her shit in a pile by the door and threw herself down on the couch and promptly fell asleep. When Carol woke up, the sun was rising, shining directly into her eyes and jolting her awake. She sat up on the couch, elbows on her knees as she pushed her palms into her eyes, she ground her face down into them, trying to wake herself up. Finally, she pushed her hands back, running them through her shoulder length hair, letting out a loud breath as she looked over at her bags and began unpacking. The apartment wasn’t huge but it was cosy, a bedroom, bathroom, kitchen and small living room.

    Carol’s first few days in Berlin were rough, it wasn’t the loneliness, she was used to that. It was the fear, at home she had her places and she'd go to them with a clear routine. Places no one looked at her funny or knew her or god forbid her mother. Here she had no clue where was safe, where was friendly. But, here, no one ever looked at her, she’d wander the streets nearly all day, everyone was busy or busy not being busy, the streets and cafes were always full of groups arguing loudly about this or that. The noise was helpful at drowning out her self pitying, the ability to disappear was even better. 

    The residency began on her fourth day in Berlin. In a run down old building, the facade littered with graffiti and signs posted in every window, most in German, one in English, “HOPE,” with Obama’s face above it. Carol huffed out a laugh and walked up the front steps, wadding through a group of twenty somethings all smoking out front and talking amongst themselves. 

    The first day was introductions, there were 10 people in the residency program, the goal being to continue developing your current work with the aid of your peers and the residency leads. Carol gets put in a discussion group with four other people, Linus a German performance artist, Mariana a Spanish poet, Miles a British autofiction writer and Eve an Australian experimental fiction writer. 

    The five of them are told to trade their pieces and spend the next hour or two reading and making notes for one another. Carol grabs a seat on a windowsill that looks down over a courtyard in the middle of the building. Occasionally she looks out the window for a break, at one point she locks eyes with Miles who sits in the courtyard leaning back on one hand, the other holding a cigarette to his lips. He stares up at her with some unfamiliar but knowing glint in his eye, he brings the cigarette up to his lips, leaving it there before he holds his hand up and wiggles his fingers in her direction arching his back playfully as he does. Carol immediately averts her eyes, uncomfortable for reasons she could but will not define.

    When the group comes back together they spend the rest of the day going over each other's pieces, they speak animatedly about each other's prose and concepts, each of them leaving with a list of at least 10 new books to read. Sometimes they get heated, Carol and Linus get into it about the proper use of a semicolon on no less than three occasions. Mariana, Linus, and Miles argue over each other, each speaking a different language about some obscure celebrity Miles has included in his story, claiming to have met them years ago (Carol makes a note to google whoever they are talking about later).

    When their first session ends Carol packs her bag, her whole body vibrating with this unbearable and humiliating excitement to return tomorrow with fresh pages.

    As she makes her way back through the smokers outside who don’t seem to have moved all day, a voice calls out to her, “Carol!” Miles jogs up to her, “Come get a drink with us! There’s this bar down the street Linus says has the perfect atmosphere for ‘literary debate’ whatever that means,” His British accent lilting as he speaks Linus’s words, tone dripping with fond sarcasm. She sees the rest of the ground standing a few paces away watching her speak with Miles.

    “Just one drink.” Carol says firmly, not wanting to be a buzz kill and also needing a drink desperately.

    Two hours later Carol has had 2 vodka sodas and is working on a third while debating with Mariana about which of Ann Carson’s books is objectively better. Mariana keeps slipping into Spanish with Carol doing her best to keep up considering her limited vocabulary. Carol feels more alive than she has in years, screaming in broken Spanish with a near stranger about books. Far away from everyone who has ever known her or will ever know her. As their conversation reaches a natural lull they return to the rest of the group, who have spread out on two couches that face each other with a coffee table between them. Linus is rolling a cigarette, one already rolled but unlit, hangs from his lips as Eve, clearly very drunk, is mumbling unintelligibly in his ear. Mariana throws herself down on the couch next to Miles who is seated next to and talking to the most beautiful woman Carol has ever seen. 

    Miles is talking animatedly, his hands moving wildly through the air in some description that Carol can’t make out. The woman is leaned forward, her elbows on her knees as she flips through some book she must have picked up off the coffee table. She doesn’t seem to be paying any real attention to Miles but is nodding her head along, her dark hair swaying with the movement, catching in the low light of the bar. She throws the book back onto the table and leans back on the couch crossing her arms, her gaze briefly returning to Miles’ but moving on as quickly.

    Her eyes stop on Carol, who has been unable to tear herself away. The woman has sharp features and cheekbones that could cut glass. Her hair is cut just above her shoulders falling in slight waves, on the right side it’s tucked behind one of her ears revealing a litany of piercings. She wears a black long sleeve button up loose on her and buttoned all the way up and a pair of matching black trousers. On her feet are a pair of healed slip-on boots. One of her sleeves is rolled up showing a delicate silver bracelet, the woman's fingers are tracing gently over it as she watches Carol watch her. But the most mesmerizing thing about her is her eyes, they’re these deep pools of warm brown, both of them so full of emotion, Carol could stare into them for hours just trying to decipher a single moment.

    Realizing that she has been staring, Carol quickly looks away and pretends to be interested in whatever Eve is trying to say to Linus who stopped trying to make sense of her hours ago. Eve jumps at the opportunity for fresh ears. Her heavy Australian accent and drunkenness along with Carol’s drunkenness making it nearly impossible to understand for either of them. While Carol tries desperately to focus on Eve she can feel the woman’s gaze burning into the side of her head. The woman hasn’t looked away once.

    Carol feels a familiar nausea rising, the feeling of being caught, the girls in the locker room all avoiding her, not wanting to “catch it” from her. She gets up and goes to the bar to escape, she downs her next drink and indicates for another from the bartender. Miles comes crashing into her side as her glass is placed in front of her and she drops a few euros onto the counter. 

    “Carol!” He screams in her ear, she flinches, it is not nearly loud enough to need to scream. “I’m so glad you came out with us, the others were too scared to ask but I knew you would come.” He says casually flagging down the bartender.

    “Scared?” Carol asks with a scoff, poorly concealing her interest. “Of what?”

    “Come on you’ve got that aloof and intimidating glare thing down. I’m sure usually no one messes with you and they just leave you alone. But I see you.” He says with a teasing smile.

    “oh, Do you Miles?” She taunts, “What is it you see?”

    He smiles at her fondly not at all put off by her harshness, “The pain,” he says simply. “We all have it.” Carol squirms at his words, she nods over the group by the couches happily chatting along.

    “They seem just fine to me.” As though she has proved something.

    “Not them,” Miles says, “Us.” Carol nearly flinches away from him and storms out the bar, but before she can, Miles lays his hand on top of hers, comforting, his eyes shine with understanding, silently asking her to stay. It is possible she has never felt closer to another person than right here at this moment. It is also possible she has never hated anyone more.

    “Whatever,” she scoffs and returns to her drink, taking an aggressive sip. Miles smiles wide at her, taking a sip of his own drink in celebration. He swirls the ice in his glass around with a straw, waiting for Carol to break the silence.

    Carol's eyes keep dancing around the bar, her drink, his drink, the bartender, the couple making out against the wall, the black cat clock that reads 2:18 am. Trying to find anything to keep her from asking about the woman, anything to distract her from her need to know who she is and why she is sitting with them. “Who’s the girl?” Carol asks, trying to put as little interest into her voice as possible, covering her mouth with her drink the moment the words escape her lips. 

    “The girl?” Miles asks, confused, turning around wildly, trying to figure out who she means.

    “Jesus,” Carol hisses, “Have some composure.” Her heart nearly beating out of her chest at his obviousness. “The girl you were talking to on the couch, she wasn’t in the residency program.”

    “Ohhh, “ Miles says finally understanding, “No that’s Zosia, she’s a friend of mine, we met in a club like three months ago and I’ve been forcing her to hangout with me ever since. She’s outrageously hot.” Miles finishes with a conspiratorial look, bragging about his friend. 

    “Sure.” Carol says before turning around and leaving him smirking at the bar as she returns to her seat on the couch next to Eve who is now speaking loudly across the table with Zosia, Linus and Mariana. Miles joins them moments later, throwing himself over the back of the couch bumping into Zosia's side. 

    “Idiot,” Zosia says to him, just barely loud enough to hear, her voice has a heavy accent. Carol isn't sure where from, maybe German? Russian? Her eyes land back on Carol with that same intensity as earlier, Jesus, now who has the staring problem?

    Carol refuses to look back, she looks anywhere else, her eyes dance among the group but never land on Zosia. Who, Carol can see out of the corner of her eye, is now leaned back against the arm of the couch twirling a strand of her own hair between her fingers with her eyes running over Carol. She can feel a bead of sweat beginning to form on her back under the weight of Zosia wide brown eyes. 

    Carol fades out of the conversation, forcing herself to take deep breaths to calm and relax her body. Maybe coming out was too much, she hasn’t spoken more than 20 words in a row, not at work, in months. It was too much, too fast, she should leave. Soon the rest of them will be able to tell and she’ll ruin the night, she should leave on a high, she has writing to do anyway. Just as she is about to stand Miles’ voice once again breaks through her barriers.

    “Zosia loves Dostoevsky!” He says in response to something Carol had not heard nor has any desire to hear. She lets out a loud laugh at the statement, making all eyes turn to her, one set weighing heavier than the others.

    “This is funny?” Zosia says, her voice low, you’d have to be inches away to hear all the subtle inflections of her tone.

    “Oh, yes,” Carol taunts. “Dostoevsky is the writer everyone likes when they just haven’t read enough books, torturing yourself with endless prose about nothing and nobody. Next you’ll say Tolstoy or Hemingway.” Carol lets out a mocking laugh.

    “Yes, I like these.”

    “Jesus, I’m going to make you a reading list. Those just cannot be the authors you like,” she looks around the group and finds them all watching with different levels of amusement. Eve nods beside her with wild enthusiasm and support.

    “Yes! I love a reading list.” Eve says, her eyes completely glazed over.

    “Sure,” Carol says with a winching grin.

    “Yes, I will look forward to this list…” Zosia lets the sentence trail off prompting for something, it takes Carol a moment to understand, as it begins to settle over her what she has just offered to this stranger. 

    “Carol,” she says, shock coloring her voice, “Carol Sturka.” Zosia stands from the couch and leans over the table extending her hand, palm up to Carol. Who refuses to stand all the way up, instead she rises, slightly bent at the knees to shake Zosia’s hand. Their palms slide against each other as their hands lock together in a firm but warm shake, a tingle shoots up Carol’s arm at the contact. Zosia leans in further, bringing Carol's hand up to her lips and pressing a gentle kiss onto her knuckles. Carol’s cheeks flush as the warmth of Zosia’s breath fans out over the skin of her hand, her heart slams against her ribs, she feels unprompted anger and embarrassment seem into her every pore. 


    “Zosia,” the brunette says as she lets go of Carol’s hand and returns to her seat on the edge of the couch, watching attentively as Carol remains bent at the knees for a moment before sitting down, her body nearly shaking with rage. Zosia has the audacity to smile, just barely, just hardly present at all, at Carol. Whose eye’s narrow and then roll, fucking europeans.

    Chapter 2: Part One: Watching

    Chapter Notes

    2005, 11 months and 2 weeks before meeting Helen, 19 years, 11 months and 2 weeks before the Joining 

    Carol’s been in Berlin for a week and a half and she has written more in that week and a half that she’s proud of than in the past year. She chalks it up to the change of pace, the new setting, the new inspiration but deep down she knows it’s because she has people around her pushing her to be better, encouraging her. The presence of a group of people she respects who actually read her work and tell her how she could make it stronger. They don’t judge her for being interested in genre or romance, they push her to write her best work possible. 

    And she pushes them back, Linus is working on a play that he wants to perform in the coming year, never having written something that needed to be polished he acts like a sponge, soaking up any advice they give him and experimenting with it. He is probably the most interested in iteration in the group, always coming in with nearly 5 different versions for them to look over. Mariana, is working on a new collection of poetry following up her book from two years ago. Her poems are sensual and seductive, messy and raw. Carol struggles the most with reading her work. Miles is working on a piece of autofiction about his life back in London, an endless stream of stories from a gay alcoholic who refuses to acknowledge the mess he leaves in his wake, Carol finds his work the easiest to critique and she refuses to examine why. Eve is working on an experimental piece of fiction about mirrors and reflections and the way people perceive one another. It is just on the edge of being totally inaccessible but leaves you with such an eerie feeling when you read it you can’t help but want more. 

    After class the five of them always go out for a drink at the same bar as the first night. Carol is a regular there now, the bartender Wolfram knows her order and can always seem to tell when she needs another. The group often goes out to dinner trying out different spots near where each of them are staying. Linus brought them over to his apartment for dinner once, as the only Berliner in the group he was the best set up to host. He and his partner Maxine live just on the edge of Tempelhof and drag them all out to the park after dinner for a walk. Occasionally, they will see films together but Miles talks too much during movies so Carol often skips these outings or just refuses to be seated anywhere near him. 

    She’s taken up smoking again as it is impossible not to, everyone here rolls their own cigarettes which she refuses to do, way too pretentious for her. So now she carries around a pack of yellow American Spirits and an american flag Bic lighter that Miles presented to her with great fanfare. 

    Zosia is sometimes present at these gatherings. Mostly at the bar, sometimes at dinner. Once she met them for a movie but she got so mad at Miles for talking that she moved across the theater from them. Carol doesn’t fully understand Zosia and Miles' relationship, the two fight like cats and dogs but later she’ll find them on a couch with Miles' head in Zosia’s lap, stroking his hair as he talks about his latest heart break. 

    After Carol gave Zosia the reading list, full of western female authors, much to Zosia quiet amusement, Carol asked, “Are you a writer?” They had been smoking with the group outside the bar, the other four running up and down the street, racing, in order to decide who would pay the bar tab. 

    “No.” Zosia says simply. Her eyes follow the chaos as the group runs by.

    “What do you do then? Are you an artist or something?” 

    “Not sure yet.” Zosia says, taking a deep drag of her cigarette. Carol stops asking there, mistaking Zosia’s short answers for disinterest and not simply the nerves that were coursing through the younger woman's body.

    Zosia is quiet, she rarely speaks and when she does it’s succinct and with a thick accent. Typically she speaks up to change the topic if something is boring her or she has no interest in it. She is not scared of sharing her thoughts, which often come across harsh or haughty like she thinks she knows best. But no one in the group seems to mind, just as they don’t mind each other's quirks, Zosia is given the same courtesy. Carol learns she is Polish one day when she overhears Miles getting her to teach him swear words, like the child that he is. She rolls her eyes at them, jealous she didn’t think of asking that first.

    There’s a certain confidence to Zosia that Carol also doesn’t understand the woman moves with purpose; her steps always sure, a few days ago when the bar was unusually crowded Carol was struggling to make her way to the bathroom, when out of nowhere Zosia grabbed Carol's hand, locking their fingers together and pulled her through the throngs of people, pushing them out of the way as she bore through the crowd. Paying no mind to the effects that her hand in Carol’s was reeking across the older woman's nervous system.

    When Carol exited the bathroom she expected to navigate her own way back but was shocked to find Zosia still there, leaning back against the wall where she left her. Her endless stare still fixed on Carol. She reached out a hand to Carol and pulled her back through the crowd toward the couches, if Zosia’s thumb rubbed the back of her hand as they walked Carol never brought it up and neither did Zosia.

    That was another thing Carol couldn’t make sense of; Zosia’s fascination with her, Zosia was always watching her, rarely did Carol’s eyes happen to glance across Zosia and not find them already staring back at her. It was unnerving. She never noticed the younger woman staring at anyone else in the group either, only Carol. It made her feel crazy, what was so different about her to merit this intensity, what was Zosia trying to figure out from watching her. Had Miles been running his mouth about her to Zosia? Was Zosia uncomfortable around her?

    At a gallery showing yesterday for one of Linus’ friends Carol had been deep in conversation with Eve and some other person they happened to adopt for the night, the three of them were arguing about art, whether it was the route of all evil or the only worthwhile thing in existence, a common theme anytime they went out and had a few drinks. During this conversation, about midway through, Carol felt her. That heavy gaze that pinned her in place, she could always feel it, like fingers gently brushing across the back of her neck sending shivers down her spine and settling somewhere low. She turned around to find Zosia, what had she done to draw her attention this time she didn’t and would probably never know.

    Zosia stood across the room at some small top table, alone and hunched forward. She was wearing a black t-shirt under a leather jacket that had seen better days, her hair was falling in messier waves than usual, tucked back behind one of her ears. A pair of loose, washed black jeans covered her long legs leading to her heeled pull on boots. Zosia’s elbows rested on the table in front of her, a thin chewed up straw poking out the corner of her mouth, drawing Carol’s eyes to her perfect lips, covered in a delicate shine, they curled up as Carol’s gaze drifted over them. She quickly looked away from Zosia’s mouth to the surface of the table where Zosia’s fingers were in the process of tearing up a napkin.

    Across her hands Zosia wore 5 different rings all silver, some more decorative, some just a simple band. As her hands moved with purpose, tearing the napkin apart, her veins flexed and popped across the surface of her skin. Her long fingers ghosting over each other as she moved the shreds of the napkin this way and that way. She tore slowly, languidly, like she wanted Carol to watch every move she made. Ripping it over and over into tiny little shreds as her eyes watched Carol intently.

    Carol watched as Miles came over and whispered something in Zosia’s ear, she said something back and off he went. Never once did Zosia look away from Carol, never once did her gaze waver. She rose to her full height as Miles left, then slowly, as if trying not to spook Carol she took a step forward, around the table. Carol immediately turned back to Eve and the stranger, praying to anyone that was listening that Zosia would not come over to their conversation.

    Carol found it incredibly difficult to carry on a discussion when Zosia was nearby, it was as though all she could focus on was the brunette woman if she got within a two foot radius of Carol's body. 

    But because the world hates Carol, Zosia did come over to them but not for long. She said she had to go, some friends had just called and she needed to go meet them. She kisses Eve on both cheeks, “Tschüss” they say to one another. Zosia turns to Carol who watches with horror and envy, she barely survived a kiss on the back of her hand. Zosia watches her for a moment, Carol watches back ready to strike.

    Slowly, Zosia relents. “Carol,” she says softly, her voice catching on the R.

    “Zosia,” Carol says back, trying to sound firm but coming out closer to a whisper. As Zosia turns to walk away, her hand brushes against Carol's lower back covered by just a thin tank top, her over shirt held in her hand, the gallery's temperature had made it too warm to keep it on. Her touch forced Carol to take a deep shuddering breath in, her shoulders and every muscle in her body had tightened. Her eyes jumped to Zosia’s for just a moment, finding them smug and satisfied. Zosia dropped her hand as she moved, her right eye closing in a soft wink of acknowledgement as she went. Carol watched her go until she disappeared across the street.

    Chapter 3: Part One: First Touch

    Chapter Notes

    2005, 11 months and 2 weeks before meeting Helen, 19 years, 11 months and 2 weeks before the Joining 

    Tonight the 5 of them (plus Zosia) are out at a bar in Neukölln after seeing Run Lola Run, a German classic that Linus insisted they all needed to see before they left. The movie's high energy and fast pace has spread across the group, everyone is clustered together talking a mile a minute. Carol feels like she is on the edge of her seat, a coiled spring ready to explode. She has taken her jacket off freeing up her arms to gesture more wildly, leaving her in a tight black t-shirt. She feels like she's twenty years old again and the world has just become hers. She moves through the night talking to this person or that person, floating on a high of energy and vodka.

    Sitting perched on the back of a couch debating with a group of Germans about the benefits or lack thereof of trains, something she secretly loves but would never admit; she’s backed herself into an argument firmly against them. Mariana stands amongst the group laughing as Carol decimates her opponents arguments, she calls over Miles to watch and the two nearly fall over with laughter as one of the Germans, throws his hands up in the air in disbelief and shouts something none of them understand before stalking off out of the bar in a huff. 

    Carol takes a big, triumphant sip of her drink, shrugging to her two friends, “So emotional.”

    As the group of disgruntled Germans move away, Mariana, Miles, and Carol begin talking about the current progress of their writing. Carol’s piece has evolved since her arrival in Berlin, she has been working on a romance novel about two people, stuck on opposite sides of the world who are mysteriously connected, but since her arrival she has struggled to find an ending that makes sense, she wants it to feel real, not some sappy happy ending, guy gets the girl bullshit that no one believes in. 

    Miles is concerned his work has no narrative, that too much of it is just clusters of unconnected stories. Mariana is concerned that her new collection won’t measure up to her first. Mariana is saying something meaningful about expectations and pressure, the impact that has on the writer or artist's ability to produce and disseminate when Carol’s attention is completely consumed by something she notices at the bar.  

    Zosia, once again is leaning against something, this time the woodtop bar, does she even know how to stand upright? She has a foot propped up on a stool rung next to her as she waits for the bartender to notice her, she watches with no anger on her face, just utterly patient, in no rush to move or be spoken to, content to observe those around her. She’s wearing a boxy black t-shirt and a pair of loose grey jeans, this time with a different pair of boots, lace-ups. 

    But what caught Carol’s eye is the person approaching her, she watches as a woman, no, a girl, stops just shy of where Zosia is leaning. Prompting the brunette to stand upright to speak with her. The girl is probably mid-twenties, wearing a pair of trendy Adidas track pants and a tight white tank top underneath a leather jacket that hits her mid-thigh. The girl's hands are covered in rings and her curly strawberry blonde hair is piled on top of her head in a messy bun that falls down in a way that seems natural but Carol knows probably took her hours to perfect. The woman moves in closer to Zosia, feigning some difficulty hearing her. Their bodies are nearly brushing as Zosia bends her neck down, to counteract their height difference, bringing the girl's lips just inches away from Zosia’s ear. Carol’s fist clenches around her drink for no particular reason.

    The girl whispers something in Zosia's ear that makes the corners of her mouth turn up in amusement. The girl takes it as an invitation and reaches out her hand, running her fingers along Zosia’s arm, which hangs limply by her side. The girl's pathetic attempt at flirting consists of her looking down at the floor coyly and then back up into Zosia’s eyes. Utterly ridiculous. 

    Carol's heart pounds as she watches Zosia interact with the girl, she’s not showing anything resembling interest, or disinterest. Carol can't tell if she wants Zosia to throw a drink in this girl's face to get her to leave faster or if she wants Zosia to return the girl’s affection. Carol can imagine it so clearly, the way Zosia hands would slowly wrap around the girl's hips moving with total confidence. Pulling the girl in closer, Zosia bending her head down further to whisper something devastating the girl's ear, her lips brushing over the tender skin she finds there before trailing lower, as the girl steadily lost the ability to speak, to overcome by Zosia to think. 

    Carol forces herself out of the fantasy? Vision? Nightmare? And tears her eyes away from them. She takes a steadying drink from her glass, returning to Mariana and Miles who seem to be entertaining themselves well enough, now discussing some show they both watch. Unable to help herself, and completely uninterested in the discussion happening in front of her. Her eyes drift back over to Zosia just in time to see her bring her hand over the girls on her own arm and gently pulling it off. Zosia says something to the girl, who smiles dejectedly in response, looking crestfallen but trying desperately to hide it. While also blushing at whatever it is Zosia whispered to her. She writes something down on a napkin and hands it to Zosia, who takes it without looking, she sets the napkin down on the bar. The girl gives one last touch to Zosia’s arm before walking away, disappearing back into the crowd.

    Carol watches as Zosia’s eyes follow the girl, her right hand picking up the napkin off the bar, still watching the girl’s retreat, Zosia tears the napkin in half. Carol feels her heart pound in time with the motion. Another tear, Carol's heart pounds and stutters, another tear, another beat. Zosia’s eyes meet Carol’s across the room, as though she could feel Carol watching. Zosia cocks her head to the side, watching Carol intently, as her fingers tear the napkin apart fully, leaving it in shreds. Carol lets out a heavy breath as the shreds fall from Zosia’s hands onto the bar top. She wonders if this kind destruction is what's happening inside her chest, is that why it feels so heavy?

    The girl was gorgeous, sweet looking, and obviously flirting with Zosia. And Zosia had shown no interest. None at all. Carol turns away, unable to look any longer, she returns to Mariana and Miles and pretends she is still breathing. Any hope that Zosia’s intensity toward her was some sort of complex flirtation is gone and replaced with emptiness. If that ‘pretty’ girl couldn’t turn her head, what hope did Carol have? A rarely out, stunted lesbian who was probably more than 10 years Zosia age. How idiotic of her to think anything different, how humiliating to think there was anything there. Because what? Zosia stared at her, Zosia stared at everyone it was her thing, always watching people with those fucking eyes. What? Because Zosia touched her back once? She was probably pushing Carol out of the way making her exit easier, asshole.

She stayed there for nearly a year; cognitive therapy, screaming, manipulation, word play, everything aimed and driven toward the destruction of Carol, all with the goal to make those around her more comfortable.

    “Send her in!” The voice calls, muffled by the closed door. 

    Carol enters the office alone. Her first observation of Val is that she’s very bright. Literally her shirt is neon pink. She wears big white frame glasses and has a mane of curly black hair. She stands from her desk and comes around it to greet Carol.

    She holds a hand out that Carol takes easily. Val wraps both of her hands around Carol’s as she shakes, “Ms. Sturka, it is so good to finally meet you.” The next thing Carol notices is that Val makes a lot of eye contact. 

    “It’s great to meet you as well,” Carol says, they are still shaking hands. “And Carol is,” Carol waves away with her other hand and clicks her tongue, “Just fine.”

    Val finally drops her hand and smiles warmly, “Carol it is,” she laughs and gestures for Carol to take a seat. She heads back around her desk and sits, she wheels her chair forward and leans in over her desk. Carol can see the copy of her book that she sent on the surface between them, probably hundreds of different colored tab sticky notes sticking out of it. “Carol.” Val says, pulling her eyes away from the tabs. “We love your book. The whole office is abuzz about Wycaro!” She’s clearly expecting a bigger reaction out of Carol.

    “That’s great I–”

    “We– Sorry Carol, please go ahead.”

    “No uh,” Carol shakes her head. “Just, that's great I’m so glad,” Carol gestures around vaguely, “That everybody likes it.”

    “Ugh!” Val exclaims, “And we do, we love it! We also have a few ideas we’d love to run by you…”

    Val goes on and on about how much they love the book, how they have big ideas about how it could be rolled out as a series, how they could see it becoming one of their flagship titles with Carol as one of their most treasured authors, she talks about advances and book tours and interviews and fan clubs.

    And Carol loves every second of it. She tries not to, she tries really hard not to. But it feels so good to be wanted, to be celebrated. Val is upbeat and freaking chipper but she talks earnestly about the book and how the story moved her, how when she finished she was angry because she wanted more so she came into work and spent four hours talking about it with Maggie.

    Carol doesn’t show how much she loves everything Val is saying, she’s doing a great job at being unreadable, her indifference only making Val want her more. Val says that she would be Carol's editor at Mammoth, that they would work very closely and build a relationship based on trust and respect so that Carol didn’t feel like Val was overstepping when she gave suggestions. Carol worries that she’d have a hard time putting up with Val’s positive attitude as an editor but she can see all the books around the room, all the book jackets out on display, clearly books Val has worked on.

    One of them is a sci-fi novel Carol read in Berlin, the sight of it makes her throat tight. She remembers the day Zosia bought it for her after dragging Carol into the book store.

    They had gone back to her apartment, it was a Saturday so Carol had nowhere to be and she guessed neither did Zosia. Carol had curled up on her couch with the book and Zosia had brought her tea and pulled Carol’s legs into her lap and let her hands run up and down Carol's legs. The touch had slowly put Carol to sleep and she’d woken up to Zosia pulling the book out of her hands and laying down next to her, she had pulled Carol’s head onto her chest and pressed her nose into the crown of Carol’s head and inhaled.

    “You know it’s creepy when you smell me right?” Carol had mumbled her voice heavy with sleep. She’d felt Zosia smile against her.

    “Go to sleep, kochanie.” She’d whispered.

    Carol had finished the book the next day and made a note to look up Mammoth Publishing later. 

    Val pulls her out of the memory, “And of course you would have your choice of manager but we have someone we think would be a great fit. She’s currently working with Titus Walbaum and Emilia Santiago on setting up their next book rollouts and we think she would just be the perfect fit to work with you.” Val pauses, her eyes shining with excitement, “Would you mind if I invited her to join us?”

    “Uh, sure,” Carol says, still dazed. “Of course.” Her hands slapping against her thighs in an awkward gesture she regrets immediately.

    Val presses her finger to a button on her phone, “Maggie can you send Helen in to me and Carol?”

    “Sure, Val! One minute.” Maggie’s voice calls back over the intercom. Val gives her another warm smile that sets Carol on edge. They sit in silence for a moment as they wait before Carol hears footsteps shuffling outside the door and then a gentle knock. 

    The door pushes open cautiously, Carol turns around to look. 

    A woman about her age enters the room. She has long brown hair and light blue eyes. She’s pretty, Carol notes absently. She wears a black sweater and a pair of flared dress pants, on her feet are a pair of sensible loafers. Tucked into the neck of her sweater, a little haphazardly, are a pair of glasses. She holds a small notebook with a pen stuck between its pages in her hands. She closes the door behind her gently. Carol instantly likes her.

    “Hi Carol, I’m Helen Umstead.” Carol stands and shakes her hand, Helen’s grip is firm around her hand. Carol squeezes back and Helen smiles. She slips into the chair next to Carol and opens the notebook on her lap. Helen clicks the end of her pen, her eyes almost nervously running over Carol’s face. “So what have I missed?” She asks playfully, eyes darting between Carol and Val. 






    2006, 18 years, 11 months and 3 weeks before the Joining. 

    Carol spends a week in LA meeting with Val, Maggie, and Helen. Mostly Helen. They spend the week talking about possibilities for the book. The different ways that the publishing house could get the book out there, the different ways that the book could be marketed to the public. They show Carol pitch deck after pitch deck. As she’s shown the third one of the week she realizes that they are selling to her. 

    Maybe it should have been obvious sooner but she had just assumed that she would have to convince them to publish her book. Not the other way around. She brings it up to Helen that night at dinner.

    This is the second time Carol has gotten dinner with Helen, the first was two nights ago after Helen brought Carol to a reading for one of Mammoth’s most recent books. It was nice, Helen is a breath of fresh air compared to the energy and youth of Val and Maggie respectively. She’s a little bit darker than them.

    Helen rolls her eyes right along with Carol at the book reading when the author gets a little too serious. Helen is also the first one to applaud when the reading ends, prompting everyone else in the room to follow. Carol appreciates her loyalty. Helen seems to respect her more as a person than the others as well. She asks Carol what she is comfortable with and what she wants out of her publishing partnership. They work well together, within a week they have written out a whole plan for the next 2 years involving an editing schedule and a rough layout of what could be Carol’s first book tour. 

    Helen says they normally don’t do this for an author until they've signed a contract but she can tell Carol needs the extra push, that Carol needs to see the whole thing before she can agree to an inch of it. Carol is almost offended by how well Helen has figured her out already.

    Carol notices that Helen is attractive, and for now that’s all it is, an observation. She doesn't have room for anything more.





    It’s Carol's last night in LA and she’s out to dinner with Helen. Carol is wearing one of the outfits she brought for the week, a beige button down with a curved collar and a pair of brown chinos, she wears the same flats as she did on the flight in. Helen is wearing a tight black long sleeved t-shirt and a pair of pleated pants. 

    “So what's the big deal?” Carol asks her, finally, after holding herself back for days.

    Helen pops a chip into her mouth, they're at a Mexican place and the tables have neverending bowls of chips and salsa. “Big deal?” Helen questions back.

    “Why is Val running herself ragged to get me to sign my contract? There must be at least a dozen manuscripts in just your two offices combined with the exact same premise as my book.” Carol grabs her own chip, it almost makes it into her mouth before she pulls it away and uses it to gesture at Helen. “I mean come on, the thing is so cheesy and so, so asinine. Any idiot could sit down at a computer and spit out something like it.” Finally the chip makes its way into Carol’s mouth. She hums around it, surprisingly pleased with the taste. Helen watches her, considering.

    “When we get a manuscript, we sometimes like to run a little test with it. We don’t tell any of them but we hand the book out to the interns, the assistants, the junior editors and we ask them to read it and give us their thoughts.” She pauses making sure Carol is still with her, Carol’s shoulders are hunched forward as she listens intently to Helen. “They all write these long drawn out reports about things that worked, things that didn’t, the ways that the books could be improved, and they all go right into the trash,” Carol laughs as she takes a sip of her margarita. Helen smiles with her.

    “What we’re really waiting for is good old fashioned water cooler talk. We’re waiting to see if they want to talk about the book, if it makes them want to get together and sit down and dive deeper into the world of the story. If a book can do that, if it can bring a bunch of people together to sit in a room and talk about it, that's a book we want to buy. Both because it will sell and because clearly, it has something special baked into it.” Helen finishes, her eyes watching Carol very closely. “Your book is still the talk of the office. Just yesterday, I heard one of the interns talking about how she was rereading it, how she couldn’t stop herself, how she had started dreaming about Raban.” Helen laughs and Carol cringes. “You wrote something that people connect with, and we can use that connection, to make a lot of money,” She smiles teasingly at Carol, hitting her hand out across the table in congratulations. 

    “Oh so it's just about the money then,” Carol sits up straighter, a sarcastic grin on her face, “All that stuff about connection and specialness it just what?” She pauses, “Fluff? Stroking my ego?”

    Helen smiles, “I’ve only known you for a weak babe and I can tell you’ve got one the size of Texas.” Carol lets a surprised smile spread across her face, she blushes gently at the teasing maybe flirty tone in Helen’s voice. 

    “Fuck off,” Carol eventually says with a roll of her eyes, taken off guard. Helen smiles back easily.

    The next day Carol packs up and heads to the airport, when she checks out, she leaves at the front desk for Maggie to pick up, her signed contract for her very first book deal. She takes a cab to the airport, they offered to get Maggie to drive her but Carol wanted to be alone. She needed a minute to just sit with all that had happened, all that had changed in just one week.

    It felt wrong how good she was feeling, how bright the future suddenly was. Her old instincts to ruin things were still there and she was still fighting them. Tchórz. But she was fighting harder now. She didn’t want to be the person who left Berlin anymore. Even if she’d never be able to go back and make things right, she could try to be better for the next time.






    2006, 18 years, 3 months, 3 weeks, and 5 days before the Joining. 

    The next eight months are busy. Carol returns to Albuquerque with more money than she’s had in years and a book full of sticky notes. They spend the next four months making changes and sending drafts back and forth, Carol gets on a first name basis with the post office staff.

    She buys nicer clothes and nicer food and nicer alcohol. She drinks less, she just doesn’t have the time anymore with her writing schedule. She and Helen call 2-3 times a week, mostly so Carol can complain about Val and her edits. Carol thinks Val is trying to push her into making Lucasia weaker, softer and she refuses to do it. Helen tells her to trust the process, that Val has done this with a lot of people and gotten more than most onto the best sellers list. 

    When they run out of things to say about Carol’s book they just talk. Carol doesn’t have many friends, none really, save for the post office staff. She’s lonely, and Helen is good at filling in the silences with chatter. She tells Carol about her days, about the other authors she’s working with, “They’re driving me up the goddamn wall. Don’t repeat a word of this Sturka.” She’ll say after ranting for 30 minutes. Carol will just laugh and say she wouldn't even know who to tell. An awkward silence always sits between them when Carol says that. Until Helen starts talking again. 

    Carol appreciates that Helen just talks, she can talk for hours about anything and they do. They talk about what books they're reading, Helen talks about how she hates LA, she talks about how she needs a vacation. Helen talks about how her mom keeps calling her and it's driving her insane. She talks about how her neighbors are fighting and it's keeping her up at night. She tells Carol about her siblings, how they keep asking her when she’s going to “settle down.” She tells Carol about when her dad died when she was 15, how her mom worked 3 jobs just to keep food on the table. Helen tells her about how she sends money back home every two weeks so her mom doesn’t have to keep working but she chooses to anyway because she doesn’t want to be dependent on anyone, even her daughter. 

    She doesn’t say any of this with heaviness, it’s just information, most of the time they're laughing as Helen tells her these things. Carol will pace on her tiny balcony and listen to Helen talk until the sun goes down, her fingers tracing over the railing as she moves. 

    Carol knows Helen is a lesbian. They’ve never talked about it explicitly but she knows. Carol is sure Helen knows she is too but she doesn’t ask. They don’t talk about dating unless it’s to make fun of other people dating, or Helen talking about how she thinks Val is fucking some other editor in the firm and Helen can’t wait for it to come out. 

    When it gets too late to maintain the pretense of a work call, one of them will hang up, saying they need to get to bed or they need to finally eat dinner. Sometimes Helen will have called her from the office and the cleaning crew will arrive and she’ll need to leave to let them vacuum her office.

    When they hang up Carol will cross her arms and smile to herself, Helen’s call sign off always making her laugh and blush, “I’ll talk to you later superstar.” Helen says it jokingly but they both know Carol loves it. Carol will pace around her apartment for a minute trying to remember everything Helen said. Then she’ll start feeling guilty and she’ll go write for a few more hours about Raban and Lucasia.

    After eight months have passed Carol finishes the book. She sends it to Val and gets an excited call a week later saying that they’re done. Carol can hardly believe it. She thought the editing would never end. “Now comes the fun part!” Cheered Val.

    The fun part was designing the book, making a marketing plan, producing the book, and then actually distributing the book. They want to release the book in September or October, an ideal slot for holiday buying, normally reserved for the big name authors who publishers know will sell. Helen fought for the slot, saying that Carol deserved it, that the final draft is one of the best books they’ve had in years and that they’d be crazy not to capitalize on it. 

    8 months after finishing the book, it finally hits the shelves. At first nothing happens. The book is out for about 2 months before it starts really selling. Carol is on the phone nightly with Helen, talking about how the book sucks and no one is buying it and that they’re going to cancel her second book option. Helen tells her to pull it together and wait for Christmas. Helen is right. 

    In late November the sales go through the roof. Suddenly every bookstore is asking for copies to keep up with demand and just about every book club dedicated to historical fiction romance novels is reading Winds of Wycaro by Carol Sturka. 

    They plan a nationwide book tour, Carol complains the whole time saying she doesn’t want to have to talk to people from Ohio, but she also pushes Helen to add as many cities as she can. She talks about how her fans are pathetic, and then scrolls on book message boards reading praise until 3 am on the phone with Helen, trading off reading comments to each other.

    When the tour starts Helen goes with her, of course as her manager most of her work has been laying the ground world for the tour, it will last for two months and span 30 cities across the US. Carol is a little nervous to spend so much time with Helen. Most of their relationship has been over the phone at this point, but she tries not to think about why it makes her nervous. She buys new clothes for the book tour, tweed because she likes the cliche of it, a few purple blouses because why not, a few skirts, all ankle length, she dreads wearing those, and a few new pairs of boots, these ones with a heel. 

    Their first stop is in New York, Carol meets Helen in the airport terminal to head to their hotel together. Helen is flipping through a magazine at Hudson Yards when she spots her. Her hair is down tucked behind her ears, and her eyebrows are furrowed as she reads. She wears a black t-shirt tucked into dark blue jeans and a pair of light brown suede boots. Next to her is a rolly bag with a smaller bag sitting on top, the two match. 

    Carol looks down at her own rolly bag, a new purchase to replace her old broken one, she also has a bag resting on top of it as it rolls behind her but it does not match, should it match? She starts spiraling about this as she approaches Helen.

    Carol wears a big black puffy jacket that goes down to her knees, a pair of nice grey sweatpants and a matching sweatshirt. Her first thought when she left that morning was that she should look nicer since she was seeing Helen but she didn’t like the implication of that so she forced herself to dress down. Her face is void of the makeup she had recently started wearing. 

    She stops in front of Helen who looks up from her magazine with fond surprise. She runs her eyes over Carol’s form, her smile twisting into something more amused. “Getting ready for a blizzard are we?”

    “It’s New York,” Carol looks around for emphasis, “In December and I’m from Albuquerque.”

    “And I'm from LA,” Helen says gesturing to her t-shirt, smug.

    “And you’ll be begging to borrow my gloves the moment we get outside,” Carol says, her head nodding along with her own words. Helen laughs. 

    “We’ll see,” Helen says before looking back down to the magazine in her hands. It’s an issue of People, with a huge picture of Prince William on the cover.

    “Jesus,” Carol says, flicking it up to see it better. “Why are you reading this garbage?” Helen gasps in mock offense, she holds the cover of the magazine up next to her face.

    “Carol! What would we do as a people, if we didn’t know that Prince William is geering up to propose to his girlfriend of 5 years and make her his princess bride!” Carol stares back at her blankly. Helen smiles wider at the annoyance on Carol's face. Helen sets the magazine down and grabs the big hood behind Carol’s head. Carol jumps a little bit as it brings them closer but she doesn't move away, her heart beats a little faster.

    Helen pulls the hood over her head and presses it down over Carol’s eyes. “Shall we?” Helen asks before grabbing her bag's handle and moving towards the exit. Carol reaches up and pulls the hood back down so she can see again, “Asshole,” she says affectionately under her breath.

    “Are you coming, superstar?” Helen calls back to her, walking backwards to look at Carol still standing by the magazines watching Helen go. She grabs her bag and follows Helen out into the cold. Helen does ask for her gloves and Carol never lets her forget it. 





    The book tour is good. It’s a lot, especially for someone like Carol who has spent the last year and a half talking almost exclusively to two people, Val and Helen. Now almost every other day it seems she spends at least 3 hours talking endlessly to strangers about her book. They seem to know more about it than even she does sometimes. At first it was charming, flattering even, now it’s just annoying. Carol knows they're just excited but it feels like they are trying to catch her in a lie sometimes. They ask endlessly about things that Carol doesn’t care about and they barely even engage in the lore of the book, which Carol thinks is the most interesting part, instead they want to talk about the love story. Carol does not want to talk about the love story. 

    She does not want to talk to Maureen from Dallas about how ‘yummy’ Raban is. 

    Carol should have prepared herself more for people asking about Raban, she should have thought about what it would mean more, to write a book about the girl who stole her heart and then put it out into the world for anyone to read. She should have thought about how it would feel to have hundreds of women, and a handful of men, come up to her and tell her they were in love with Raban. She should have realized how angry that would make her, how resentful it would make her. 

    The most common question on the book tour is who was her inspiration for Raban. The first dozen times Carol flinched when it was asked. Helen beside her had noticed and redirected the question, telling the readers in a conspiratorial voice, “Don’t ruin the fantasy ladies!”

    She would grab Carol's shoulder reassuringly and keep the crowd moving. 

    Helen is amazing on the tour, she takes care of everything before Carol can even say something is wrong. She’s the first to clap when Carol finishes reading every time, she keeps the lines moving when Carol signs books, and she shit talks after with Carol in the car. 

    Helen is also different on the book tour. Not when it’s the two of them, but just like Carol, when they go to different bookstores Helen changes her look. She wears more feminine colors and cuts of fabric. She pulls her hair up into a bun on the back of her head. She wears dangly earrings. At first Carol had been unnerved by it but she noticed that Helen mostly did it when they were in smaller or southern cities, in the more major metropolitan areas Helen would dress like usual, t-shirts and jeans, a sweater and a watch, loafers. 

    Carol watches Helen transform her personality depending on where they are and who she is talking to, the sarcastic and teasing edge replaced by an almost maternal warmth that the readers respond to immediately. A firm tone that leaves no room for question when the reading or the signing is over and people won’t stop talking to Carol. A gentle hand rubbing a stranger's back when, every now and then, a fan gets overwhelmed by the excitement and starts crying. Carol watches it all in admiration, she wishes she could turn it on and off the way Helen can. As it is now, the tour is wiping Carol out and they still have one final leg left, 5 cities remain in the southwest before the tour finishes in Albuquerque.

    The constant pressure to be warm and welcoming wears on Carol who does not naturally exude warmth or welcoming. She watches Helen though and tries to mimic as best she can, Carol learns how to smile around the 5th city on the tour, she learns what tone to speak to fans in around the 8th. Around the 15th she learns how to dress. 

    While Carol watches Helen, Helen watches Carol. She watches how Carol engages with the fans earnestly about theories and how she remembers even the most obscure details from her book and is able to weave these stories about their origins that delight whoever she is talking to, Helen isn’t sure that any of them are true but who cares if they make the fans happy? She watches Carol’s eyes light up when fans bring up a sequel, she watches Carol jot down notes on receipts and napkins and paper scraps when she has an idea mid conversation and has to get it down or she’ll forget. She watches the light in Carol’s eyes shine brighter when she disappears into this world she has created and she wonders if Carol has ever let anyone inside it with her. 

    They talk about Carol’s ideas for the series, how she could see it going even beyond a trilogy, if the sales keep up. Helen watches Carol pretend she doesn’t love the book, like it doesn’t make her happy and fulfilled that so many other people love it. She watches Carol judge herself and her readers. She watches Carol flinch when anyone asks about Raban. She watches Carol turn herself into someone else that she thinks her readers want her to be. 





    They’re in Seattle now, they’ve just finished a reading at a Barnes and Noble, Helen promised Carol a drink at the hotel bar after because the crowd was being particularly difficult. Question after question about Raban. Carol and Helen were both working their hardest to dodge or deflect but it didn’t help at all. Carol almost cracked halfway through and Helen must have been able to tell because she announced they were taking a break, brought Carol into a back room for some deep breathing and promised her that afterwards they’d get a drink together in the hotel bar if Carol went back out to finish.

    So there they sit, Carol with a martini and Helen with a jack and coke. Helen is making light of someone's obsession when Raban as Carol rolls her eyes and plays with her hair watching, slowly pulling out bobby pins and laying them on the bar. Carol has been waiting for Helen to ask about it. The tour is almost over and Helen has shown great restraint in not asking Carol what her deal is about the character she wrote. But it seems that’s all coming to an end now. 

    “Come on Carol,” Helen shakes her arm, begging. “An old friend?” Helen is trying to guess who Raban is based on. 

    “Nope,” the ‘P’ popping. Carol takes a long sip from her glass.

    “An ex-boyfriend?” Helen asks, her voice cautious but leading.

    Carol laughs, “Nope.” the ‘P’ popping again as she grabs a peanut out of a bowl and starts tearing it open.

    Helen watches her for a moment, her eyes softening, “An ex-girlfriend?” Carol’s shoulders tense at the word, she swallows the peanut in her mouth and takes a sip of her drink. She purses her lips in Helen’s directions and says:

    “Hardly.” Helen huffs out a warm laugh, then her face gets serious, like she wants to cut through Carol’s bullshit as quickly as possible.

    “Come on Carol,” Helen says gently. “I read the first draft. You did a good job switching things up but a few of those ‘she’s’ and ‘her’s’ got left behind before they could be turned into ‘he’s’ and ‘his,’” Carol’s eyes jump up to Helen’s in fear.

    “Does Val–” Helen puts a hand on her arm. 

    “No,” Helen shakes her head, “Everyone just assumed it was an error.” She laughs, “I think Val could walk into a gay bar and say ‘wow everyone is so close’ before she’d realize where she was. I don’t even think she knows I’m gay and we’ve worked together for 6 years now.” Carol tries to smile, always enjoying when Helen makes fun of Val, but it doesn't meet her eyes.

    “God,” Helen says, sitting up a bit straighter. “She really did a number on you huh?”

    Carol swirls the olive around in her glass, “More like I did a number on her.” Carol grumbles, her eyes darting to Helen’s almost braggy, but not that.

    Helen smiles as she leans back in her chair and crosses her arms over her chest, her eyes cutting right into Carol’s, “You dog.” Helen says her eyes squinting in mock judgement.

    Carol lets a genuine smile form on her lips as she takes a sip from her drink.





    Helen stays with her after the tour finally ends in Albuquerque. Carol sees no reason not to invite Helen to stay with her. Six months ago Carol had moved into a nicer apartment in a nicer neighborhood. It’s a three bedroom apartment with an in unit washer/dryer, a dishwasher and a huge private balcony. The AC works every night and the electricity doesn’t flicker. She has one of the bedrooms set up as a home studio, the walls covered in notes for the next Wycaro and one corner is overflowing with paints. Carol started painting again before the tour. She hadn’t painted since high school art class but that classroom held some of her only happy memories from childhood so she decided to pick it back up. She had decided she couldn’t just spend her days writing, calling Helen, and drinking.

    Carol turned the second bedroom into a guest room, she wasn’t sure why she made a guest room when she had no one to host but maybe the idea was sparked when she found out the tour was going to end in Albuquerque and that Helen would need somewhere to stay. The third bedroom is of course hers. 

    After the last reading ends, Carol and Helen go out to dinner to celebrate. The tour was a ringing success, the sales for the book are through the roof and fan clubs have started popping up across the country, about halfway through the tour Val called saying that they were working on sourcing merchandise, “the real way to make money,” she had said a little darkly. Carol and Helen had both laughed about her tone later. 

    They go to a restaurant Carol has never been to but has been wanting to try for years now. It’s a nice place, the prices a year and a half ago would have made Carol walk right out the door, but now she doesn’t even blink. It feels good. Carol feels good. She feels, maybe, happy. 

    Her and Helen get a little drunk together over dinner, they get a little loud with their laughter but neither of them care. Helen makes Carol take a cab home with her, to convince Carol, Helen has to promise to buy her breakfast in the morning and to come with her to grab her car. 

    When they get back to Carol’s building they stumble into the elevator, leaning into each other to stay upright from how hard they are laughing at the faces the cabby had made at them in the rearview mirror, Carol’s body tingles where they touch. They find their way into Carol's apartment, she drops her keys into the little bowl by her door. And raises her hands out to the room in front of her, “Welcome to my home,” Carol says like a showman. 

    Carol falls back against the wall of her entryway where Helen is already leaning, watching her with something heavy in her eyes. “What?” Carol asks with an oblivious smile. Helen drags herself closer to Carol, Carol assumes to whisper some stupid joke into her ear as always so she leans in closer to Helen.

    She’s shocked when Helen presses her lips against hers. She somehow was not expecting it at all. Carol doesn’t pull away. She kisses Helen back and it feels so good. Helen's lips are soft and tender and they move so confidently against hers. Carol moans into the kiss, feeling a year and a half’s worth of tension leave her body. It’s been so long since she’s been kissed, since she was touched.

    The thought makes her whole body freeze against Helen's. The last person who had kissed her was Zosia. The last person who had touched her was Zosia. And now it's Helen. Carol feels her last connection to Zosia snap like the stitches on her heart are tearing. Her lips freeze against Helen's and she thinks she might cry. The loss is so overwhelming. She barely even remembers what Zosia’s lips felt like now. A week ago she had seen a pack of polish cigarettes and hadn’t felt like throwing up and that lack had made her want to die.

    Helen pulls away from her, concerned and instantly sobered up. “Shit.” Helen says, stepping back, her hand running down her face. “I am so sorry,” she laughs at herself. “That was so wildly unprofessional.” Carol is still frozen. “I can stay somewhere else, I can get someone else assigned as your manager when I get back to LA it’s no problem.”

    Her words pull Carol from her haze. She grabs one of Helen’s hands in hers and pulls her out of her panic. “No,” Carol says. “It’s fine, it’s not, it's…” Carol decides to be honest, Helen deserves it. “I wanted to kiss you.” Her eyes run over Helen's face, trying to let her eyes speak for her. “It’s just…” she trails off, losing the words.

    Helen picks them up, her hand squeezing Carol’s, “Your ex.” She says simply. Carol doesn’t speak for a moment, she doesn’t let herself deny it for once. And then she’s laughing. She pulls her hands out of Helen's and brings them to her hips as she laughs, Helen watches bemused. Carol puts her hands over her mouth as she leans back against her wall and slowly stops her laughter. She rolls her eyes at herself and bites at her lip, annoyed. “God,” she shakes her head, eyes coming back to Helen’s, “What a fucking lesbian cliche I am, huh?” Helen laughs with her and leans back against the wall next to her.     “...So yeah we’re…” Carol stumbles on the word, she looks to Helen whose face says something like, you’re so stupid (affectionately) I’m not helping you. Carol rolls her eyes back, her heart fluttering, “Together. Like together.” She finishes lamely but then worries it’s too vague. “Like dating. We’re dating.” Carol gestures between herself and Helen. 

    Val stares at them silently for a moment, her eyes darting between the two of them. “You’re gay?” She asks Helen first, shocked. 

    “All my life,” Helen says with a smile and a raise of her hands that says ‘guilty.’ 

    “Wow, I’m stunned.” Val’s using that voice that straight people have when they find out someone is gay and they can’t believe it because the person is ‘pretty.’ “But, I mean, like, so happy for the two of you!” She leans forward and puts her palm against the surface of her desk. 

    Carol has no clue how Val had no idea they were dating or even a suspicion they were dating. Helen has been coming to visit Carol in Albuquerque pretty much every three weeks for the last year and Carol has been coming to LA just as regularly. Carol has been on a phone call with Val and called out to Helen asking her to clarify something and said, “Thanks baby.”

    When they come to the office together, they arrive and leave in one car. Carol is currently wearing a sweatshirt she stole from Helen with ‘EMERSON’ across the front, a school Carol did not attend but Helen went to for her graduate degree. Straight people.

    After the shock wears off, Val asks Carol if she’s going to ‘come out.’

    “No, God no,” Carol says, almost cringing. Helen laughs, amused by her reaction not offended at all. “I mean not like,” Carol gestures broadly with her hands. “Publicly, to the readers, but you know, here, in this room and others, yes, sure.” She pauses suddenly, as if she hadn’t fully realized the weight of it. “I’m out.” Helen grabs her head in hers and brings them up to her lips, smiling proudly at Carol who smiles back. The idea of pulling her hand away from Helen doesn’t even enter Carol’s mind. 





    2012, 14 years, 1 month, 3 weeks, and 4 days before the Joining. 

    Between her second and third book Carol takes an extended break. Her and Helen are just coming off her second book tour and it was rough. The second book’s writing time was almost twice as long as the first because Carol was worried it wouldn’t be as good and she kept making changes. It drove Val insane, but the book did even better than the first. It was Carol’s first best seller, she was number 8 on the list two weeks after the second Wycaro was released. 

    The tour was huge, almost twice as long as the first, dozens of interviews, meetings about the film rights, hours signing books and t-shirts and tote bags. The lines at every reading were twice as long but the questions were still all about Raban.

    When they finally got back home, Helen had moved in just before they left, they were both bone tired. Helen suggests Carol takes a break and Carol agrees almost immediately and is amused by the shock on Helen’s face. Carol wants to just exist for a little while, she doesn’t want to spend weeks tearing her hair out trying to think of an idea for book three. She wants to enjoy her life, she wants to enjoy Helen. They decide to travel for a while, this time on their own schedule. They go to as many places they can afford on Helen’s bucket list and spend as much time at each as they can. They do not go to Berlin. 

    Carol complains the whole way around the world and Helen stares at her with stars in her eyes while she does. They fight like everyone else. Carol’s complaining can turn nasty sometimes, Carol’s fear can lash out to draw blood. Helen can sometimes forget that she isn’t just Carol’s manager but her girlfriend too. And Helen lashes out too, mostly when Carol says things about how Helen’s family doesn’t deserve her, a sore spot for Helen. Helen’s mom has started refusing to cash the checks that get sent to her every month. 

    Carol met Helen's mom once when they were passing through on the book tour. It did not go well. Carol spent the whole time biting her tongue because of the way Helen’s mother spoke to Helen. She did her best but Carol could only hold herself back for so long before she snapped back. Helen had been pissed afterwards, but after the anger faded it gave way into sadness. Sadness that her mother couldn’t just say she was proud of her, sadness that Carol saw how she let her mother speak to her.

    They spent the night laying in their hotel room watching Golden Girls reruns. Carol brushing her fingers through Helen's hair, as her head lays on Carol’s chest, and rubbing her back. Carol presses her hands harder into Helen’s body every time she feels it shake with laughter, trying to encourage Helen to laugh harder. Wanting to help relieve as much of the tension she felt in Helen’s shoulders as possible. Helen had held her so many times just like this over the years. Through freak outs about negative reviews, drunken crying when the memories got too vivid, moments of panic that she was unworthy of the life she had built, and worst of all after phone calls from her mother. 

    Carol’s mom calls every now and then. Carol does her best not to pick up but sometimes she looks without thinking or sometimes she's in the mood for pain. It always ends the same. Her mother tells her what a disappointment she is and how her books are filth and she’s living in sin with that woman. Helen finds her every time and holds her just like this. Her fingers running through Carol’s hair, silently expressing all that love and care and tenderness that Carol was deprived of for so long. 

    Carol’s almost relieved to be able to see Helen like this. It makes her feel less alone, it makes her feel less broken. That someone like Helen, who is so strong and so sure and so seemingly comfortable, can be hurt just like her, but still get up the next day and carry on and find the good in the world. It makes Carol proud, so fucking proud, to be with Helen. To be around Helen. To get to feel that strength. To get to maybe be part of what makes Helen so strong. God, she hopes she's a part of it. Carol hopes she provides even a fraction of the comfort Helen provides to her on a daily basis. 

    They were already in love and had said it ages ago at this point. But when Carol looks back on their life together, this is the moment she knew she wanted to spend the rest of her life with Helen.

     

     

    Carol still drinks, that never really goes away. She drinks less but her favorite coping mechanism is still opening up a bottle and even though she has Helen there’s still so much to cope with. Helen doesn’t bring it up as much as past girlfriends had, maybe because Carol does drink less now, but sometimes Helen’s eyes will linger on a drink in Carol’s hand too long or she’ll count how many drinks Carol has had on a night out and they’ll spend the drive home in uncomfortable silence.

    Sometimes Helen will suggest they do a dry month and Carol will laugh and say, “Have fun.” Carol will walk out of the room and hope to god that Helen won’t follow her to keep talking. Most of the time Helen doesn’t follow her.

    Soon Helen will start refusing to buy alcohol for Carol when she does the grocery shopping. 

     

     

    One night Carol goes out with some friends, they’re a newer group of friends, Carol met them at the golf club she had joined when they got back from the tour. It’s a group of lesbians about her age who mostly use the club as a bar that they sometimes play golf at. Carol goes out with them one evening, Helen had been so excited for her, she didn’t show it of course but Carol could tell Helen was giddy at the idea of Carol having a group of friends. When Carol left that night she felt like Helen was watching her like a parent watches their child walk into their first day of school. It had pissed her off and made her feel sad and pathetic and maybe she drank a little more than she should have because of it.

    Carol doesn’t really remember much of the night except for her arrival. She had had fun, she remembers that, but things get blurry and then they go black. 

    When she woke up, she was in a hospital bed, an IV in one arm and handcuffs around the other. There were a few small cuts around her body and a broken collarbone from her seatbelt but otherwise she was fine. She had watched through the window of her room as Helen talked with the police. She couldn’t hear what Helen was saying or what the officers were saying, but she watched as Helen talked and the harsh faces of the cops softened, soon they were nodding and shaking hands with Helen. 

    When they came into Carol’s room, they took the cuffs off and Helen took the freed hand in hers while the officers walked out of the room. “You’re lucky to have her,” one of them said to Carol about Helen as they left. “Don’t waste it,” The other said as the door closed behind them. Carol wanted to call out about how they were pigs but she thought better of it with how scared Helen looked beside her. 

    Helen brought Carol’s hand up to her lips and pressed a long firm kiss into her skin, a few tears slipping out of her eyes. “You fucking asshole.” Carol stares back, for the first time in years feeling fear about Helen leaving her. “You could have died,” Helen holds in a sob. Carol pulled Helen into the bed with her, ignoring any pain she felt as she held Helen in her arms while she cried. Carol tried to comfort her but she didn’t know what to say. Even then she knew she wouldn’t stop drinking. Instead, she just rubbed her hand up and down Helen's back and whispered as comfortingly as possible into her hair, “I love you, I’m not going anywhere, I love you.” Over and over again until Helen's shaking subsided. 

    “I don’t know what I’d do without you,” Helen had said, her voice so scared. 

    “You’lll never have to find out,” Carol had said back before she pressed their lips together in a desperate kiss. 

     

     

    A week later the interlock breathalyzer is installed in their car, Helen's deal with the cops. The DUI stays out of the public record and Carol has to blow into the device anytime she wants to get behind the wheel. Helen mostly drives them after that.

     

     

    A week after they sign documents for a domestic partnership, marriage wouldn't become legal in New Mexico for two more years. When Carol was in the hospital they’d only let Helen back to see her after Helen said she was Carol’s manager.

    They both secretly feared this wouldn’t be the last time Carol found herself in a bed and they wanted to be more prepared for the next time. They made it romantic, two nights in a hotel after they signed the papers with an all inclusive spa out in the desert. Carol stayed sober for it because she knew it would make Helen happy.





    2018, 7 years, 4 months, 4 weeks, and 27 days before the Joining. 

    Carol publishes her third book after a long drought, they had worried some of the hype would have died but it seemed people were just as desperate as ever for Carol’s mindless pirate stories. Stormshadow of Wycaro was the biggest book yet, the time away made the existing fans ravenous for more and gave time for knowledge of the book's existence to spread, giving it a whole new audience when the book finally hit the shelves.

    Before the book's release and the book tour she and Helen decided to freeze her eggs, just in case they want to have kids in the future. Carol wasn’t totally sold on the idea but she loved the thought of having a family with Helen someday enough to go through with it. Helen would always tell her, “it doesn't mean we have to use them, it just gives us the possibility for the future.”

    Carol dreams of their future together. Carol loves her life with Helen, she loves waking up with Helen, she loves showing Helen a new chapter she’s written, she loves watching Helen pull on her reading glasses and the face she makes when she does. She loves bickering with Helen about nothing, she loves the way Helen’s mouth curls around the word Asshole when Carol is pissing her off, she loves the way Helen doesn’t judge her, she loves the way Helen makes her feel comfortable in her body. She loves that they can talk to each other after all this time with just a look and the other instantly understands. She loves Helen’s body and her lips and her eyes and her hair. She loves the way Helen talks to her during sex, she loves the way Helen touches her. She loves the way Helen makes fun of her and teases her and pushes her forward. She loves the way Helen protects her from the world and makes Carol believe she is worthy of that protection.

    Carol knows that she doesn’t fully let herself enjoy these things, that she still has more fear than no fear. That she still flinches when she sees someone's eyes linger on her and Helen too long. That her mother and the counselors and the ex-girlfriends words still ring in her ears when she gets too comfortable. But she knows Helen doesn’t mind, she knows Helen understands. She knows that she has time.

    Carol knows they aren’t perfect, but she doesn’t want it to be perfect, she wouldn't be able to trust ‘perfect.’ She just wants whatever Helen can give her and she wants to give everything she can back.





    Sometimes Carol thinks about Zosia. Less and less as the years go by, but still… sometimes.

    It’s hard not to really, she wrote a whole book series about her for god's sake. Her reader's favorite question is still asking who Raban is based on or who Carol pictures when she writes about Raban and Lucasia.
