Carol knew that if she stayed, it was only a matter of time before her fragile sobriety was shattered and her grief overwhelmed her. 

     

    The house had been impossible to part with. Carol refused to sell it or have someone rent it from her. She kept it empty; she paid people to clean it once a month and she lied to herself that she would go and spend a few weeks there every year. Carol had Helen’s body exhumed before she left. Manny had come back to Albuquerque and stayed with her in a hotel while that happened, Carol couldn’t sleep that night and Manny stayed up with her, playing cards and arguing over allegations of cheating. 

     

    She had Helen cremated just like she always wanted. Helen told Carol when they were in Norway that she wanted her ashes spread up there, underneath the Northern Lights so that she could be a part of them. Carol hadn’t made that trip yet, she wasn’t ready to say that final goodbye. Instead, she sat on Carol’s new mantel in the most expensive urn Carol could find. It was a beautiful mahogany box with floral patterns engraved by hand on its surface. Carol would stare at it some nights in anger, mad at the world that the woman she had planned to spend her life with could somehow be contained within such a small box, so small that it fit above her fireplace. 

     

    Her new building was a classic Chicago greystone, her apartment the second and third floor of the building, it was much bigger than she needed but she liked having the room to spread out, she had gotten used to the size of the house and she didn’t want to feel crammed into some shoebox apartment when she could afford to live comfortably. 

     

    Her street was quiet, it was mostly families and young people who thankfully kept the noise pretty minimal. She had only one neighbor in her building, a relief after years of cul-de-sac living, his place was the basement and first floor of the building. Carol had met him a few times but she wasn’t planning to go ask him for a cup of sugar anytime soon. His name was Jack, he was nice enough, probably in his late forties, she thinks he works in advertising but Carol has never asked him and never will ask him. She knew he was gay, both from the little flag he had in his window and the revolving door of men she would often pass by when she came and went. 

     

    She liked the building and she liked the street. She liked living in a city she had no history in, a city that she could make her own.





    When she finally makes it home that night, Carol is dead tired, the walk and the reunion with Zosia has drained every bit of energy she felt after the reading. She can barely even remember the high from the bookstore, the reading feels like a lifetime ago now.

     

    She makes her way up the front steps of the building, her feet heavy on the stone as she struggles with her keys from how frozen her fingers are. She pushes the door open and takes a deep breath, relieved to finally be somewhere warm. She closes the door and turns to make her way up the stairs.

     

    When she turns around, Carol is immediately confronted with the image of her neighbor, Jack, shoving his tongue down the throat of some random guy in their hallway. Carol quickly averts her eyes to the floor and walks by them, briskly, towards the stairs. 

     

    “Holy shit,” a shocked voice behind her says. “Carol?” 

     

    Carol stops on the first step and takes a calming breath, preparing to deal with a fan or just someone grateful for the ‘whole world saving thing,’ who doesn't respect personal boundaries. She looks back over to Jack's door, the last person on earth she would expect to see, stares back at her with an excited grin: Miles.

     

    Carol’s annoyance fades into surprise and she immediately straightens her spine and removes any remnants of tiredness from her face, not wanting him to be able to see how much this night has worn on her. 

     

    Miles looks good. His hair and short trimmed beard are sprinkled with a healthy amount of grey, he has new lines around his eyes, and a smattering of new freckles on his cheeks; he’s wearing a tight black t-shirt and pleated, black pants into ankle boots. In one of his hands he holds a wool jacket with a black fur collar. 

     

    When Carol’s shock and fear wears off, she feels unexpectedly happy. A hesitant smile forms on her lips to match the big grin spreading across Miles’ face. She missed him.     “Why would she get a tattoo of my initials?” Carol asks after a moment, dreading the answer, her heart feels like it's being squeezed in a vice. 

     

    Zosia just looks at her. The same way Zosia looked at Carol when she first arrived more than a month ago. The same way she looked at Carol when she asked why they had sent Zosia. That telling empty silence and those apologetic, sad eyes, fill the space around the two of them and Carol feels her stomach drop. She wants to cry, she looks at Zosia and all she sees is pain and heartbreak and her own failures. 

     

    She leans forward and presses her lips to Zosia's, her hand still on the side of her face. She moves her lips against Zosia’s trying to press an apology into her lips. Carol can feel how much Zosia loves her in the way she moves and she chooses to believe it's this Zosia, not the other one. It’s easier.





    “She didn’t say anything, she couldn’t say anything that would hurt me,” Carol looks away, guilty, hating herself for being so weak, “So, she didn’t say anything at all.”

     

    Miles sits in silence for a moment before he moves upright in his seat, “Well,” he clasped his hands together, “I was with her when she got it. We were celebrating and saying goodbye, Zosh was leaving to get her Masters and I wasn’t quite done with Berlin yet so I was staying behind.” Carol flinches at the nickname. “We got drunk, we went clubbing, we got more drunk, and when we were walking home we passed a tattoo shop, Zosia decided she wanted to get something to remember Berlin by.” 

     

    He grabs his water off the table and takes a sip, Carol listens with wrapt attention, “First, she was going to get a bear but she thought that was too expected. Then maybe it was going to be the name of the street she lived on but she hated Lukas and Tobias by then and didn’t want to remember them every time she saw it.” Where did she go for her masters? Carol wants to ask—but doesn't. Why did she hate Lukas and Tobias? Carol wants to ask—but doesn't. 

     

    “You’d been gone for 6 months at that point and Zosia hadn’t talked about you in almost 4. Even right after you left it was like pulling teeth getting her to talk about you. She just refused to do it. And there was the whole…” he trails off, gesturing vaguely with one hand, “Anyway.” Carol wants to stop him, she wants to stop him so badly, but she can’t. She’s still hiding from herself, still hiding from him. “The point is, she hadn’t talked about you in so long. But I knew her and I knew she was heartbroken, obviously, she was heartbroken. I knew she missed you and that she was still in love with you. I knew already that she probably always would be, it’s just how she is I guess.” He looks away, a fond smile on his face, Carol hates him for having so much history with her. She hates herself for being so jealous.

     

    “She went into the back with the artist and came out about an hour or so later, I was bouncing off the walls still drunk and in a partying mood.” He laughs, “I begged her to show me but she just blushed and refused to. After about an hour of pestering she finally lifted her hair and showed it to me: CS.” He smiles sadly over at Carol, she can tell from his eyes his sadness isn’t for her, it’s for Zosia, “I think she thought I would judge her for it, think it was pathetic or something.” 

     

    He crosses his legs again and brushes off some non-existent lint, Carol sees a moment of resentment towards her flash across his face and it disappears just as quickly. “She’s always struggled with that,” he scratches at his beard, trying to be casual but Carol can feel the point of his words like a knife to the stomach. “Feeling like an idiot after you left but even worse, I think,” he pressed a hand into his chest, “Was her hoping that you would come back.”

     

    Carol’s eyes dart away and land on Helen’s urn. She’ll never regret it. She’ll always regret it. She’ll never regret it.

     

    “I hugged her and told her it was beautiful and she cried for the first time since I had known her.”

     

    “I barely knew her,” Carol says, a tear rolling down her cheek. 

     

    “I know,” Miles nodded. 

     

    “She barely knew me.”

     

    “I know.”

     

    “I was cruel to her,” Carol says, her voice shaky. “I refused to speak to her, I refused to touch her,” she scoffs at herself. “Half the time I could barely even look at her. I had never met a person who seemed so comfortable with who they were, so secure in want they wanted and what they thought.” Carol swallows the knot in her throat, “She never hesitated, not for a moment, never questioned any of her choices, never worried she had made a wrong move. She just knew herself.” Carol wipes tears off her face and sits up, needing Miles to hear her, needing someone to hear her. “And I abandoned her.”

     

    “I let her fall in love with me, and I knew the whole time that I would leave and never speak to her again.” She swallows hard, “I liked how much she wanted me,” She licks a tear off her lips, “How much she loved me. No one had ever wanted me like that before and it felt so good.” She closes her eyes at the memory, “I soaked up every bit of love and affection she gave me, she made me feel better, she made me feel worthy, she made me feel like I deserved a life, and then I left her.” Her red eyes stare hard into Miles’ gentle ones. “Without so much as a word,” Her voice rising, “I don’t deserve her loving me and I never did.” Carol spits out the final words, her self-disgust so painfully present.

     

    Miles sits up slowly and leans forward, “It’s not about if you deserve it or not, Carol. She loves you.” He tilts his head to the side, his eyes full of compassion and understanding, “And you love her.” His voice wraps around the words like a prayer and Carol feels them like a brand against her skin. “She deserves to hear you say those words back to her—at least once. Whatever she does with them after that,” he purses his lips. “That’s her decision.”





    Miles left a few hours later, he’d hugged Carol at her door and before he left he’d pulled out a notebook, wrote something inside, tore out a page, and passed it over. Ten numbers stare back at her. He had given her an appraising look, told her not to disappear again, kissed her on the cheek, and walked out the door. 

     

    Carol had gotten ready for bed. She showered, she moisturized, she turned off the lights around her apartment and settled under her covers. She stared at the numbers propped up on her nightstand for almost an hour before she typed them into her phone and wrote the following message:

     

    It’s Carol. Miles gave me your number (long story). We should talk. Again. Sent 3:38 am

     

    Please? Sent 3:39 am

    Chapter 15: Part Two: Confession

    Chapter Notes

    hello all!

    things got a bit out of hand as I wrote the final chapter and its taking much longer than I expected because it is outrageously long lol, I've decided to break it up into three chapters and 19 will be an epilogue.

    I'm going to upload once a day until they are all out, enjoy :)

    2029, 4 years, 7 months, 3 weeks, and 17 days since the Unjoining.

     

    A week passes before Carol hears anything. 

     

    She sits at her desk and types out meaningless words that she can’t focus on and her ears strain to hear the imagined ding of her phone. She leaves her apartment to get groceries or to get air and she stares at the faces of the people around her looking for Zosia. She calls Manny and tells him about what has been happening and then can’t hear a word he says after because she starts spiraling about what she would do if Zosia texted back while she’s on the phone with him.

     

    What if Zosia tried to call her while she’s on the phone? Carol should hang up as soon as she can just in case. Carol explains as much to Manny who sighed loudly, “Do you even know what you want to say to her?” He asks exasperated.

     

    “Of course,” Carol says immediately. “It’s all I've been able to think about for days now.”

     

    “Well?” He prompts.

     

    “Well what?” 

     

    “Say it to me,” he says annoyed.

     

    “Why would I do that?” Carol wraps her arm not holding the phone around her stomach defensively, her fingers tapping against the cotton of her shirt.

     

    “Because I said so.”

     

    “Dick,” she mutters. They are both silent for a second, she can hear him breathing into the phone, waiting. She kicks a leg out at a pillow of her couch that slowly falls to the floor as she delays. She moves her foot underneath it and flips it over, entranced by the way it moves and in love with the way it keeps her from speaking.

     

    “Carol,” he breaks the silence with a soft voice. “I think if you are going to try and… and speak with her again, you need to know what it is you want from her.” He takes a breath and Carol drops her chin down onto her chest, “Otherwise it may be,” he pauses before continuing, “unnecessarily cruel.” 

     

    “Cruel?” She sneers. “You think I’m cruel?” 

     

    “No. I think that you are impulsive and maybe a bit inconsiderate.” She scoffs offended by his words and waits for him to take them back or apologize, he doesn't. 

     

    “This pep talk sucks,” Carol bites out flopping down onto her couch. 

     

    “You are my good friend Carol Sturka. I care for you but you are rude and you are impulsive. This woman, she has been waiting seemingly 25 years to hear what you have to say to her, I think she deserves more consideration than you figuring out what to say ‘in the moment.’” Carol protests his words by staying silent. “What is it that you want from her?” He asks confused, “Do you want her forgiveness? Do you want her understanding? Do you want her love?”

     

    Carol swallows hard, she has no clue what she wants from Zosia. That’s always been the problem hasn’t it? She doesn’t know what she wants from Zosia, she doesn't know what she can ask for from Zosia—especially not now, after everything. 

     

    In Berlin and in the years after, Carol had let herself forget or deny how much Zosia loved her. She hadn’t really believed it, why would someone like Zosia love her? What did she have that Zosia couldn’t find in someone else? Someone who wasn’t so rude and impulsive and scared. Why her? There was nothing special about Carol, she had some talent as a writer, she wasn’t ugly, she wasn’t bad in bed. But she was—difficult. 

     

    She had always been difficult, her mother would berate her for hours asking why she couldn’t just control herself and her urges. Her mother would ask her why she couldn’t be more like Heather, the girl in the church choir who spent her weeks volunteering and wearing tennis shirts and had a perfect delicate cross around her neck. Her mother would ask her why she couldn't sit like a lady or be more agreeable. 

     

    The counselors would smile and tell Carol she just wasn’t trying hard enough, that if she really loved her mother, that if she wanted to be accepted or find someone who would love her she needed to change. They would tell her about how horrible her life would be if she didn’t change, and how she would live in a lonely empty world if Carol didn’t change. 

     

    Her ex-girlfriends would tell Carol she was going to die in the closet if she didn’t stop feeling so ashamed, that she’d spend her life watching as the world moved around her and wither in the shadows. They’d tell Carol her mother was gone, that she was free, that she didn’t have anything to be afraid of, that the world was different, that people were different. But Carol knew that people didn’t change, they just pretended to. 

     

    But then there was Zosia, the first person who never asked Carol to be anything she wasn’t. She never pressured Carol to change or be softer or gentler. She would watch Carol argue with Miles or Mariana and get this hungry look in her eye. She would watch Carol as she tried to hide herself in a crowd and wrap her hand gently around Carol’s, she’d hold Carol's hand softly in hers giving Carol room to pull it away, telling Carol with her eyes ‘I see you.’ 

     

    Zosia who never pretended to be anything she wasn’t. She would roll her eyes and groan loudly at the people around her. She listened intently to the people she cared about and she would drop everything to make a friend happy. She would stare at Carol without fear. She never questioned who she was and that was all Carol seemed to do. Carol didn’t understand how someone like Zosia saw anything of value in Carol. She didn’t believe that a person like Zosia could love her. Zosia was perfect. And Carol was… something else.

     

    Carol had let herself think it was just the heat of the moment in Berlin when Zosia said she loved her, she let herself think it was just lust that Zosia stared at her with; maybe Zosia just had a thing for older women.

     

    She let herself think that Zosia had forgotten about her. Carol always prided herself on her self-awareness, her painful awareness of who she was. But when it came to Zosia, there was a wall she had sealed herself off from. 

     

    She couldn’t let herself think about the truth. That she had broken Zosia’s heart when she left, that Zosia would have happily spent her life by Carol’s side. That if Carol had returned to Berlin and apologized, Zosia would have forgiven her almost immediately. She let herself not think about and not ask about the tattoo after she saw it. She blocked out Zosia’s words from the ski lodge and pretended they didn’t exist. 

     

    But now, with Zosia and Miles' return, the wall is starting to come down and Carol can’t deny the truth any longer. Zosia has been in love with her for 25 years and maybe Carol loves her back. 

     

    “I don’t know what I want from her,” Carol whispers into the phone, ashamed of herself. 

     

    “Well,” Manny sighs. “Clearly she is taking her time to respond, so you have some time to figure it out.” Always so rational. 

     

    “What if what I want,” Carol takes a deep breath. “What if what I want isn’t what she wants.” She feels like a toddler, kicking her feet on the couch, her eyes roaming around the room in fear. 

     

    “It might not be,” he says gently, Carol’s eyes feel heavy. “I think if I was her,” Carol laughs softly at the idea and the fact that Manny is talking to her about this, he is clearly uncomfortable and unsure but he’s doing his best. “I would feel quite embarrassed.”

     

    “Embarrassed?” Carol scoffs, “Zosia doesn’t get embarrassed.”

     

    “If I was abandoned by my lover and never got over her, and was then sent to her without a filter or without being actually present and I told my lover that I had a tattoo of her initials, that I never dated anyone else, and then my best friend told my lover that I was still in love with her, even after all of that.” He takes a deep breath in, “I would be quite embarrassed—even if I had no shame, I think I would quickly find some.” 

     

    Carol doesn’t know what to say to that. 

     

    “I think that you need to figure out what you want and whatever that may be, you need to tell her what it is. Even if you may be rejected or denied whatever it is you want. I think it is your turn to be embarrassed.” He’s quiet for a moment, “Maybe vulnerable is the better word.” His voice is insecure at the weight of Carol’s silence. 

     

    “What if I can’t do that?” Carol asks again, her voice small, she turns onto her side as she whispers the words into the phone, curling into herself.

     

    “You will do that.” He says firmly, “because you are not cruel and it’s what she deserves.”



    ————



    So a week passes until Carol hears anything and she spends that week thinking of what to say if Zosis ever decides to respond. 

     

    When Carol hears from Zosia, she has finally stopped checking her phone every hour and she feels more sure of herself than she has since they first saw each other on the street.

     

    Zosia’s response is simple:

     

    1881 Sheridan Road room 205. Tomorrow at 5.





    The sound of the car door closing behind Carol makes this whole thing feel a bit more real. The drive had been a silent affair. The driver quickly realized that Carol was not interested in chatting but instead interested in anxiously staring out the window and tapping her foot against the floor of the car. 

     

    She had to be driven to the address Zosia sent, it was too far to walk, the building when Carol looked it up was Harris Hall, a part of Northwestern’s campus, the history building. She would have respected Zosia’s ability to put Carol on such a back foot if the choice hadn’t made her so unbelievably nervous. She assumed Zosia picked it because of this reason. A sort of home field advantage.

     

    Carol stepped away from the car, staring at her phone, double checking if she had the right building—its probably the 10th time she has checked. The building looks old, it’s stately. The front lawn is well manicured with symmetrical shrubs and plants on either side, matching the symmetrical design of the building. It’s three stories with bigger windows on the first and second floor, there is a small staircase up to the entrance and a wrap-around ramp that was clearly added later.

     

    A few students stand a few paces away from Carol on the curb, smoking in a little circle, huddled together like penguins for warmth. Carol stares up at the building with a sense of foreboding. She isn’t really sure what to expect from this meeting, she doesn't like not knowing what is about to happen. 

     

    But every minute that passes brings her closer to 5 and she doesn’t want to be late. Zosia had always been very intense about punctuality and the last thing Carol wants is to start off on an even worse foot. She climbs the stairs hesitantly, the door at the top bursts open as a handful of students stream out, laughing and calling back to each other as they pour out of the building. The last one to leave holds the door open for Carol who takes it with a polite nod and a deep breath. 

     

    The interior of the building is just as impressive as the outside, the walls are a warm cream color or just full on dark wood paneling. There are chairs lining the halls and bulletin boards hung every few feet, students mill about the building reading or typing on their laptops outside of closed classroom doors. There is a directory dead ahead that Carol approaches, about midway down the list is RM. 205 - Zosia Kolakowski. Carol’s hand almost comes up to trace over the name before she remembers where she is and she shoves her hand back into the pocket of her coat. Carol hadn’t really been sure what to wear to this… meeting. She had settled on her standard black puffy jacket and matching black gloves. Underneath she had a white button down with the first two buttons undone and a white tank top under it and a pair of worn in blue jeans she had had for years. On her feet were a pair of heeled boots. She wasn't sure what the etiquette was when going to see your ex… whatever who you played house with during a global hivemind to confess your love. 

     

    And that is what Carol was here to do. Confess her love to Zosia. 

     

    Carol had spent the week trying to think of what she wanted to say to Zosia, why did Carol text her? Why can’t Carol just let her go? Why does she keep pulling Zosia back in?

     

    Carol spent the week thinking about Zosia and about Helen. Carol knew that if Helen was still here that she never would have stepped foot in this building. It wouldn’t have ever crossed her mind to seek out Zosia again. She would probably be in Albuquerque still writing the Wycaro books, or maybe the hivemind would still be around. 

     

    But whatever that world or that reality that still contained Helen was, it wasn’t this one. 

     

    Helen was gone and she wasn’t coming back.

     

    There had been a moment—after Manny and Carol had saved the world—just a moment. Where Carol almost expected Helen to come back with everyone else. She knew it wasn’t going to happen, she knew it was different, but for a brief moment, she had expected to walk back into the house and find Helen waiting for her.

     

    When Carol didn’t find her there it was like she lost her all over again. Or like the loss had only finally sunk in. The last year and a half since the unjoining has been full of grief, Carol even tried to go to a few support groups for people who lost someone in the joining but she found everyone there so annoying and self indulgent and Carol never went back. 

     

    It still didn’t seem fair that Carol had saved the world and still Helen was gone. Carol spent the last year and a half angry, she would always be angry, she would always be grieving and always be wondering what her life would have been. Would Helen and her have used the eggs they had spent so much to save? Would they have spent their whole lives together, happy and in love? Would Carol have ever stopped hiding?

     

    Carol sometimes found herself walking down the street and hating every person who walked by her, every person who lived through the joining was worthless in comparison to Helen. Why should they still be here when Helen is gone? Why had Carol worked so hard to save them when Helen was gone? Carol should have left them to rot. 

     

    Those are Carol’s bad days. 

     

    On her good days, Carol walks down the street and she holds her head up and the people around her nod at her in polite thank you’s for saving them and she thinks that if Helen is watching over her, from wherever the fuck, she is proud. 

     

    Carol knows that if Helen was still here, she would not be in this building walking towards Zosia’s office, that she never would have sought Zosia out at all. That fact alone almost made Carol call this whole thing off, it felt unfair of her to know that and to still try to pull Zosia back into her orbit. Like Zosia was some sort of consolation prize.

     

    Carol also knew that Zosia probably knew all of that too, and that made her stomach turn even more. She knew that Zosia had all of Helen’s memories and that Zosia knew how much Carol had loved Helen, Zosia could see twenty years of unwavering dedication from Carol. Twenty years of love confessions and intimacy and vulnerability that she was never given by Carol. 

     

    Carol wondered what that felt like for Zosia, it must be horrible, it must be the worst thing in the world to have all of that in her head. She must be tormented by it, Zosia’s fear that Helen had something she didn’t made more and more sense the longer Carol sat with it. 

     

    But Carol also can’t help but wonder—does Zosia sort of like it? 

     

    Was it comforting? 

     

    If Carol was in Zosia’s shoes, if she had spent the last two decades of her life pining—she sees an image of Raban in her mind—and was then given memories of those two decades from the point of view of Zosia’s partner; it would be horrible, it would be devastating, it would be comforting, would it not be like she had almost there herself? 

     

    Does it feel like Zosia was in the room for their first meeting? For their first kiss? For their first I love you? Does it feel like she was in Helen’s body or is it more like she is watching from the corner of the room? Did Zosia go searching through their life together for traces of herself? There were so few moments when Carol would speak of Zosia but she knew Helen had thoughts and feelings about Zosia that she didn’t share; and now Zosia knew those thoughts and feelings.

     

    Carol knows she wouldn’t be here if Helen was still alive, but Helen is dead and she is walking down the hall of the building Zosia teaches in. Helen is gone and she isn’t coming back. Carol could spend her life in mourning and she probably will, but does she need to spend that life alone? Does she need to continue punishing herself for things out of her control?

     

    When Carol left Albuquerque she had promised herself and she had promised Helen that she would try to stop hiding from the world, she would do her best to join it. Carol wandered the streets and she went to restaurants alone, she got rid of her dresses and her skirts and she stopped trying to smile politely when she wasn’t feeling polite. 

     

    She admitted to herself that she loved Zosia. Carol had loved Zosia in Berlin and she had run from her. She wouldn’t do it again. She couldn’t. 

     

    Carol didn’t know what Zosia would say. She didn’t know if she would be angry or glad but Carol knew she had to tell her. Manny was right; it was Carol’s turn to be brave and to be vulnerable.

     

    She moves down the hallway of the second floor scanning the room numbers as she walks, her steps echoing off the linoleum of the deserted floor. She finds the room easily enough, it’s the third door from the left, its dark wood with a glazed window pane, there’s a light on in the office but Carol can’t see through well enough to make out anything else. She takes a deep steadying breath that comes out in unequal bursts; and knocks.

     

    Nothing happens for a moment, so Carol knocks again, still nothing. She checks her phone for the time 4:55. Carol turns her head looking both ways down the hall before trying the door knob. It doesn’t budge. She licks her lips in frustration and considers the possibility that Zosia has just decided not to come. 

     

    But then she hears an echo of applause from down the hall. It's quiet but on the empty floor any sound carries down the length of the hall, Carol follows the sound to large double doors in the center of a long hallway, one of them is cracked open and as Carol approaches and a few students exit the room. Two girls walk past her holding each other's arms, flushed and whispering, their blushes deepen as they catch Carol watching them leave. Carol turns her head to watch them go, confused by their behavior before her gaze is pulled inside of the room. 

     

    It’s a small lecture hall with probably only 6 rows of seating with aisles down the center and on the sides of the room. Students are slowly grabbing their bags and putting on their jackets. Some are furiously scribbling notes. There is a group of probably 8 students clustered around the front of the room and Carol can’t make out what they are clustered around. Behind the group is a small raised stage, probably 3 feet off the ground with wood floors and two chairs set up facing each other. 

     

    Her eyes are drawn back down to the group at the front of the room as one of the girls shifts, Carol can see around them to Zosia. Carol knew it would be her, how could it be anyone else? Zosia is leaning back against the edge of the stage with her arms crossed, listening intently to one of the students in front of her. Her eyebrows pressing together in concentration and her face serious as she nods along, the student speaks steadily turning more and more red as she continues on. The group around Zosia is completely ignoring the girl who is speaking in favor of casting furtive glances towards their professor and to each other. 

     

    Carol crosses her arms over each other as she moves to the side of the open double doors and watches. She doesn't blame the students for their blatant obsession. Zosia is responding to whatever this girl was asking her, Carol can’t make out the words but she can hear the low hum of Zosia’s voice from where she is standing. Zosia is speaking with such passion, her hands moving as she gestures, her fingers tucking fallen hair behind her ear and missing a strand that lingers in front of her face. Every word that leaves her mouth makes her jaw look sharper and the skin over her cheekbones pull tighter. Her mouth slowly pulls up into a smile as she finishes whatever she is explaining and the group around her lets out a loud laugh followed by nervous giggles. 

     

    Zosia is wearing a tight, knit polo with buttons all the way to her waist, it has lighter colored vertical lines, and the top two buttons undone. The fabric is folded over itself exposing the skin just underneath Zosia’s collar bones. Against that exposed skin sits a thick gold chain to match the rings that cover Zosia’s fingers. Her hair falls around her chin in gentle waves and is tucked behind an ear, haphazardly, on the right. She's wearing a pair of black, wide leg trousers that shift in a way that makes Carol tighten her arms around herself as one of Zosia’s hands slips into her pockets. On her feet are a pair of black leather boots with a thick sole. 

     

    Carol watches as another professor who she hadn’t even realized was in the room enters the conversation and shakes Zosia’s hand, Zosia nods along with her and the students around them all call out goodbyes as this person moves to leave. They turn down the center aisle making their way to the door and toward Carol. 

     

    Zosia, still at the front of the room, watches this person leave and her eyes settle on Carol. Her eyes run over Carol's body making Carol’s spine tingle before coming back up to her eyes. Carol watches as a small, familiar smile spreads across Zosia’s face and almost lets one cross her own before Zosia jerks her head away—clearly she had not meant to smile—and back to the students around her.

     

    Carol watches Zosia as she begins excusing herself from her little gaggle of fans and wonders why the smile on Zosia’s face felt so familiar. Zosia was always the type to smile more with her eyes than her face. Her smiles were rare and loud when they broke through. This was gentle, her eyes had shined back at Carol for a brief moment in a hauntingly familiar way.

     

    As Zosia moves up the aisle towards Carol she recognises the expression. It's the smile Helen used to give her after readings. The face she would make when she was proud of Carol for pushing through her discomfort and showing up. The gentle curve of her lips that Carol still found herself looking for in every room she entered. 

     

    “You’re early,” Zosia says, stopping a foot away from her. Carol uncrosses her arms and pushes her hands into the pockets of her jacket. Her eyes wander around the room behind Zosia finding the students in the room trying to hide their staring. 

     

    “Better early than late right?” Carol jokes. Zosia just hums in response, she stares at Carol for a moment longer before turning back to the room. 

     

    “Thank you all, I will see you on Monday.” Zosia calls out with a gentle wave, a chorus of ‘bye professor’ and ‘thank you professor’ calls back to her. She walks by Carol and doesn’t look to see if Carol is following, of course she does, not wanting to linger with the gossip eyed students. Zosia’s boots click on the tiled floor and Carol does her best to keep her eyes from roaming over Zosia’s back, the shirt is tight across her shoulder blades. 

     

    Carol leans against the wall next to Zosia’s office as the brunette pulls a key ring out of her pocket. “No carabiner?” Carol jokes, her voice mocking, her nerves manifesting in nervous sarcasm. Zosia’s eyes dart over to her and Carol wishes she was dead. Zosia slips a key into the lock with one hand, her eyes running down Carol's body to her waist where a silver carabiner is attached to Carol’s belt loop. Zosia reaches out with her free hand and wraps her hand around Carol’s keys. Carol can’t help but talk a step forward, swallowing something heavy in her throat and feeling her thighs clench together. Zosia’s eyes dart back up to hers and Carol can see the smug, satisfied gleam in them. She jingles the keys attached to Carol’s waist loudly, jarring Carol out of her haze.

     

    “Too loud,” Zosia says before dropping the carabiner and pushing her office door open, she walks inside as though nothing had just happened and Carol is frozen where she stands. She can feel the ghost of Zosia’s hand tugging against the keys on her hip and is fighting the urge to press her hand against the skin there to either replace or seal in the feeling. “Carol,” Zosia’s voice calls out impatiently. 

     

    It startles her into motion, she steps into Zosia’s office, closes the door gently behind her, and turns to face Zosia. The office is small but cozy, there are three lamps spread around the room making the office warm and intimate. The walls are lined with shelves of clearly barely organized books. The floor is covered in more book piles and random piles of loose sheets of paper. There are a few pieces of art hung on the wall, they all look like old posters from eastern europe or from New York in the 90s. Zosia’s desk is to the right of the doorway with two arm chairs across from it. One of them is covered in a pile of fabric, Carol can only guess it is Zosia’s coat.

     

    The desk is similarly a mess of paper, with pens scattered across the surface, Carol can see old coffee rings staining the wood and the corner of a laptop buried under a pile of looseleaf paper. Carol smiles as she takes in the chaos around her. She remembers weaving around the piles of clothes in Zosia’s room and the justifications Zosia would make for the mess. Carol remembers the way Zosia would fold her clothes and make neat little stacks every morning during the Joining 

     

    Carol much prefers this mess. There’s a window next to Zosia's desk and an ashtray on the sill beneath it. Carol huffs in amusement as she steps deeper into the office, she pulls her coat off, and throws it on top of Zosia’s on the chair and sits in the one beside it, across from Zosia who watches her intently.

     

    “Is that legal?” Carol asks, pointing to the ash tray.

     

    “Why?” Zosia asks, her eyes never moving away from Carol. “You going to tattle?” Carol blushes again and is grateful for the soft lighting that probably hides the worst of it. They fall into silence after that, Carol’s nervous haughtiness falling into insecurity just as quickly, her eyes moving in the space around Zosia’s head, never looking directly at the woman across from her. And Zosia stares right into Carol. “Miles told me about your reunion,” Zosia breaks the silence, her voice heavy, she picks up a pen and spins in between her hands. 

     

    Carol sniffs and crosses her legs, “Yeah,” she turns her head scratching at her ear in discomfort. “That was unexpected, to say the least.” Zosia just hums, waiting.

     

    Carol knows Zosia is waiting for her to speak but she suddenly can’t remember a word she was going to say, it had all seemed so easy in the abstract, but now with Zosia in front of her, with Zosia looking even more perfect than she remembers in front of her, she can feel her nerve slipping and the words she had planned have vanished.

     

    “What did you want to talk about?” Zosia asks, her voice tense as she speaks, maybe angry. Carol tries to tell her she’s sorry with her eyes but Zosia’s doesn't soften at all. Carol looks away, embarrassed. Tchórz. 

     

    25 years and she still can’t face her. She’s still hiding in the dark of the hallway while Zosia lays in her bed staring at the door. 

     

    Carol closes her eyes for a moment and steadies herself. If she leaves here and Zosia doesn’t want her she won’t be alone. She has Manny. She won’t be alone, she won’t be alone, she won’t be alone.

     

    She turns back to Zosia. “I wanted to apologize.” Zosia’s eyes flicker with surprise. “For Berlin.” Carol rolls her eyes at herself, enough with the vagueness. “No, not for ‘Berlin,’ for leaving you.” Carol leans forward slightly, the pen keeps turning in Zosia’s hands. “I don’t regret it.” The pen stops, “I can’t.” Carol’s voice almost whispers, they both feel Helen in the room. “But I was selfish, I left without thinking about you and what it would be like for you after.” She laughs darkly, “Hell, I existed in Berlin without thinking about how any of my behavior affected you.” Carol pushes a finger into the arm of the chair, “I still don’t really know how it affected you.” She looks up to Zosia who is watching her stonefaced, her body still as a statue. “Will you tell me?” Carol asks tenderly, eyes full of hope as she looks up at Zosia through her eyelashes.

     

    Zosia jerks just barely in the chair, almost imperceptively small. She drops the pen onto the surface of the desk and raises her eyebrows before leaning on the desk with her elbows. “What good will that do now?” 

     

    “I think, maybe, that you need to say it to me.” Carol says cautiously, “You seem… quite angry.”

     

    Zosia barks out a laugh at that and Carol smiles as well, looking down to hide it. Zosia licks at her lip, her teeth biting into it as she stares at Carol. “I knew you were scared in Berlin, of me and…” Zosia gestures between them. “You flinched when I touched you and you would look around the room or the street anytime I kissed you.” She picks the pen back up, elbows still on the desk, lost in the memory as she speaks, she watches the pen turn in her hands. “I suppose I thought if I just showed you,” her eyes jumped up to Carol’s and away. “Showed you how I felt, that it would be enough.” She rolls her eyes at herself. “I was young and I was stupid and dealing with my own shame too.” Carol wants to ask what that means but she doesn't want to interrupt.

     

    “When you left, I wasn’t surprised, I expected it. I barely slept that night, I was waking up every hour to check and see if you were still in bed next to me, it was like I could feel you were about to leave.” Zosia’s eyebrows press together, her face pinches up as she speaks. “The leaving was bad but worse was you not telling me, finding out from Linus in front of everyone.” Zosia takes a breath. “That would happen.” She stares up at Carol, hurt shining in her eyes, “I would be talking to Miles or Linus or Mariana and they would tell me things about you; where you grew up, where you went to school, what your book was about.” Zosia scoffs, “And everytime I would hide my surprise and pretend I knew these things because I didn’t want them to know that I didn’t.” 

     

    Carol swallows heavily in her chair. “Why didn’t you say anything?”

     

    “What would I have said?” Zosia scoffs, “What would you have let me say?”

     

    Carol looks away in shame, “Nothing.” She stares out the window, she thinks about Sarah, the girl she had loved at 16 who Carol never spoke to again after drunkenly climbing into her bedroom.

     

    “What is it you want, Carol?” Zosia says angrily. “I appreciate your apology and you finally asking me how I feel, but what do you want? Why did you ask to see me again?” Her voice rising in volume. “To get into another fight, to sling our mistakes at each other one more time?” The veins in her neck are straining against her skin. “Is this fun for you? Are you looking to write a sequel?”

     

    “No,” Carol says weakly, heartbroken at the idea that that’s what Zosia thinks of her. “I’m sorry. I didn’t mean to…” She trails off, gesturing in frustration with herself. “Look,” Carol sighs, leaning forward, setting a hand on the desk gently, as though it was a part of Zosia herself. “I am an asshole and I am difficult and… I am who I am.” Carol’s hand presses into her chest, her nails biting into the fabric of her shirt. “I wrote the first book because I hoped that you would see it and that you would understand that I was trying to apologize and that I was trying to find a way to forgive myself and I was trying to find a world where I felt like I could love you because it felt so impossible to do it in this one.” She hears Zosia take in a harsh breath as she speaks. 

     

    Her eyes dart back up to Zosia, who stares back at her with a failing mask of indifference. Her eyes are always a dead giveaway to how she’s really feeling, they're so full of pain when they look at Carol now she can barely see anything else inside of them. 

     

    “I loved you.” Carol says firmly, without hesitation, without difficulty. “I loved you then, I loved you in the joining.” She takes a calming breath, her heart racing. “I love you now.” Carol’s vision starts to blur as tears well in her eyes and she sees matching tears gathering in Zosia’s. “I have no right, and you can tell me to fuck off and you’ll never see me again if that’s what you want and I’ll understand but I needed you to know because I never said it in Berlin and I need you to hear it. I love you. I always loved you, even when I didn’t know how, even when it felt impossible, I loved you.”

     

    “Why are you doing this?” Zosia asks, her voice broken, she wipes away a tear before it can fall. “Why can’t you just let it go? Just let it go.” Zosia pleads, Carol can hear the ‘me’ Zosia omits.

     

    “Because,” Carol says, shifting closer to the desk in her chair. “You think I didn’t love you, and I’ve let you live with that thought for far too long.” Carol looks into Zosia’s pain filled eyes and she sees her broken heart staring back at her. “Miles can say you haven’t moved on and maybe you haven’t, I don’t know.” Carol pauses, her eyes slipping closed in pain, unable to look at Zosia as she speaks. “But maybe… maybe you just think you aren’t good enough, because over and over again I rejected you and made you feel unwanted and unloved and like something to be ashamed of.”

     

    She looks up at Zosia who has slowly started curling in on herself as Carol speaks, silent tears rolling down her cheeks and the curve of her jaw. Carol gets out of her seat and walks around the desk. Zosia’s eyes widen in panic as Carol moves, she tries to push her chair away but Carol grabs the arms of the chair and keeps her seated. Carol pulls the chair closer and sinks to her knees in front of Zosia who can’t help but lean forward, bringing them closer together. 

     

    “You're incredible,” Carol’s voice was full of wonder. She brings her hands up to Zosia’s face, holding it, brushing Zosia’s tears away with her thumbs. “I love you,” she says it again, unable to stop now that she’s started. “I’ve spent the last week trying to figure out what to say to you and that’s all I’ve been able to come up with. I love you, I think about you all the time, I want to be with you.” She presses in closer to Zosia, their foreheads touching. Zosia’s breathing heavily against her, the breath puffing out against Carol’s lips. 

     

    Carol leans even closer, her nose brushing against the skin of Zosia’s cheek, Carol’s eyes slip closed focusing on the feeling of Zosia’s body so near to hers, Zosia’s lips so near to hers. Carol stops there for a moment, waiting for Zosia to push her away, waiting for Zosia to speak. When Zosia doesn’t move Carol does, she leans her head up further needing to feel Zosia’s lips against hers.

     

    They ghost over Zosia's; Carol feels the moment they touch like an explosion, barely just a brush of their lips together and already Carol is gasping out against Zosia. She wraps her lips around Zosia’s bottom lip and for a brief moment she thinks Zosia is about to kiss her back. Zosia raises her arms up toward Carol and she thinks for a moment she is about to be pulled closer.

     

    But instead Zosia’s hands take hold of Carol’s elbows and she pushes her away with a sob. Carol’s hands stay holding Zosia’s face, their foreheads stay pressed together as Carol falls back and watches Zosia cry. Her breath came in short gasping bursts, her eyes pressed shut with tears caught in her eyelashes. 

     

    Zosia’s hands come up to Carol’s head and she holds her close, before Carol can pull back her fingers threading themselves into Carol’s hair. “I can’t,” Zosia whimpers out, her voice so soft and broken that Carol can barely hear her. “I can’t do this again. I barely survived the first two times. I can’t be your safety blanket because Helen is gone. I can’t.” She almost sounds like she is begging but Carol isn’t even sure if the words are directed at her.

     

    “That’s not what this is,” Carol says, her hands tightening around Zosia, trying to merge her hands with Zosia’s skin because she can sense she is about to be pushed away.

     

    Zosia pulls her head back and opens her eyes, they’re red and glossy as she stares down at Carol and the sight of them, the sight of her, the heartbroken look on her face and the devastation in her eyes makes Carol want to scream. “And how am I supposed to believe that? How am I supposed to trust you?” Zosia says angrily. “Why would I ever believe you? I won’t be some second choice, consolation prize you use to comfort yourself in your loneliness Carol.” Carol’s chest tightens, “And I won’t spend the rest of my life wondering if you love me or if you love that version of me they sent you.” 

     

    Carol reels back in shock at the words, she wants to run away, she wants to get drunk and hide from Zosia and never see her again. Maybe that would spare Zosia this horrible pain that Carol keeps bringing back to her door. Maybe if Carol left and Zosia never saw her again she would be able to finally heal.

     

    But that’s bullshit and Carol knows it. That would only make her feel better, that would let her hide from herself and from the world and pretend that she is the honorable one when she leaves Zosia to deal with the fallout of her own actions. Carol won’t abandon her again. She’ll stay and she’ll face the music and she’ll face the pain she has caused and she will stay. 

     

    In front of her Zosia has started crying again, her confession too much for her to take, her shoulders shaking. Carol sits up and wraps her arms around Zosia and holds her while she cries. She rubs her hand over Zosia’s back and holds her, feeling Zosia’s tears seep into her shirt, and Zosia’s face bury itself in Carol’s neck. 

     

    “It's okay baby,” Carol whispers in Zosia’s ear gently. “It’s okay.” Her hand rubs gently against the length of Zosia’s back as she fights off tears and sobs of her own at the sight of the woman she loves so broken. She can feel the roughness of Zosia’s scar through the weave of her shirt's fabric. Carol’s eyes slip closed in pain and she feels a tear slip out of her eye, she’s made so many mistakes. “It’s okay baby,” she whispers again as Zosia’s body begins to relax against her, her shoulders shaking less against Carol’s, and her breathing beginning to steady. 

     

    Carol pulls away, holding Zosia’s face close to hers, pushing Zosia’s hair back behind her ears and wiping tears off the younger woman's face with the sleeve of her shirt. She runs her thumb reverently over Zosia’s cheekbone and holds her gaze, “I’ll wait.” She presses her lips into Zosia’s forehead, they move against the skin there as Carol speaks, “As long as it takes for you to believe me or even if you never believe me, I’ll wait and I’ll show you.” She kisses Zosia’s forehead again, holding her lips against her skin trying to memorize the taste and prepare herself for the reality she may never get to press them against Zosia’s again. “I love you,” Carol says again, her voice cracking around the words. 

     

    One of Zosia’s hands comes up and wraps around Carol’s wrist, she doesn’t push her away she holds Carol’s arm in her hand and they stay like that for a while, holding each other, breathing with each other, neither of them wanting to pull away but knowing the moment will come eventually.

     

     

    Chapter 16: Part Two: Who are you?

    Chapter Notes

    one step closer to the end :'( not nearly ready to say goodbye to these two

    I finished the epilogue today so the berlin au is official finished, can't wait to get the rest of the story out!

    2029, 4 years, 8 months, 3 weeks, and 17 days since the UnJoining.

     

    They didn't see each other for a month after that. Carol spent the first week desperate to text Zosia but she held herself back. She went to AA meetings, she walked around the city, she read on her couch, she made dinner, she called Manny, she wrote at her desk, and she thought about Zosia. She lived her life and after a few weeks she realized she felt, okay? Not good or bad, she felt content, she knew she would hear from Zosia again and Carol was willing to wait for that moment to come. She didn’t feel the need to rush towards it. Carol wanted Zosia to come to her when she was ready. She wanted the choice to be as firmly in Zosia’s hands as it could be.

     

    That didn’t mean Carol wasn’t going a little crazy. She spent hours over the course of the month combing the internet for information about Zosia and had been shocked by how much she was able to find. Papers she had written, videos of her lecturing, photos on instagram (Miles’ account, Zosia didn’t seem to have one), a whole paper trail of Zosia’s life was on the internet and Carol devoured it all. 

     

    Carol watched every lecture she could get her hands on. The oldest one was from ten years ago. Carol had to pause it after just a few seconds after Zosia started speaking. Her accent was still thick, just like it was in Berlin, and her voice still caught around certain words. Carol had slammed her fingers into the space bar to pause the video and stared at it for almost a full five minutes trying not to be so pathetic as to cry at the sound of Zosia’s voice. 

     

    Carol would watch that video 17 times over the course of the month, a few times while making dinner, a few times while she cleaned her apartment, and one shameful time she tried not to think about, with her hand moving between her legs and her back arching off her bed. 

     

    When Carol does hear from Zosia again it’s through Miles. He texts her:

     

    Z and I are going to trivia night at Dorothy’s. Sent 3:20pm

     

    Care to join? Sent 3:30pm

     

    She could practically feel Miles’ glee at leaving a ten minute gap between his messages, knowing that Carol would be eagerly waiting for any word from him. She thinks about making him wait but the only one who would be annoyed by that would be her.

     

    Does she know you are inviting me? Sent 3:33pm

     

    Of course. Sent 3:35pm

     

    What time? Sent 3:36pm

     

    7. Sent 3:36pm

     

    Wear something hot ;) Sent 3:36pm

     

    Carol had thrown her phone away in anger and then immediately picked it back up to call Manousos. She held a hand over her eyes as the phone rang in her ears, knowing already that he would be no help but needing someone to be there to witness her breakdown. He answered after a few rings and listened silently as Carol explained why she was calling. When she finished he muttered something too quietly in spanish for Carol to hear and asked what she needed. 

     

    They had switched to Facetime and Manny did his best to help Carol sort through her wardrobe. He would shyly avert his eyes while Carol changed from shirt to shirt and pant to pant. A faint blush on his cheeks as he watched Carol throw piece after piece onto the floor of her bedroom. Carol would stare at him expectantly as she tried every new combination of her clothes and roll her eyes when he would say the same thing every time, “good.”

     

    Finally, after almost two hours, Carol found something she liked. Carol stared at herself in the mirror as Manny walked into his kitchen to make a pot of tea. “You look good, Carol Sturka,” he says, watching Carol turn this way and that way in the mirror. 

     

    She settled on a black t-shirt that was tight around her arms, showing off the new muscle tone unlocked from her sobriety, and tucked it into a pair of black, straight leg cargo pants. Carol knew that when she sat down or if she raised her arms over her head the shirt would pull out from the waist of her pants and she knew that Zosia wouldn’t be able to resist looking. 

     

    She pulled on a black collared long coat that stopped at her knee and turned back to Manny, her phone propped on her bed against a pillow. She picks her phone back up and gives him a grateful smile while he playfully rolls his eyes at her, making the smile wider. “What are you doing tonight?” Carol asks with a laugh.

     

    “I guess I'll be trying to get a few hours of sleep before you wake me up in the middle of the night to talk about your evening,” he says all of this without an ounce of sarcasm and Carol doesn’t argue against him, knowing the odds of that happening aren’t zero. 

     

    “Well then,” she cocks her head to the side, “I guess I’ll talk to you later then.”

     

    Manousos laughs, “Buenas noches, Carol Sturka.”

     

    “Buenas noches, Manousos Oviedo.”





    The car drops Carol outside the bar, the building is painted dark grey, almost black, warm brass lights illuminate the sidewalk around the windows and the front awning is covered in a blend of white, purple, pink, and green flowers. There are a few people on the right side of the building smoking. They look up at Carol as she closes the car door and give her a once over. She feels herself stand a bit straighter, confidence blooming in her chest as she steps toward the door.

     

    “Carol!” A voice to her left calls out.

     

    Her neck snaps towards it, Miles of course, and Zosia beside him. He has a light brown, knit cap on his head, not even covering his ears with how haphazardly it’s placed on his head. A lit cigarette hangs between his lips as he waves her over and holds an arm out to welcome her. 

     

    Zosia’s welcome is much more subdued, Carol approaches them as Zosia watches with an unreadable expression, exhaling a cloud of smoke into the cool night air. Miles wraps his arm around Carol and she returns the gesture, grateful to have him here as she feels Zosia’s eyes boring into her. Miles’ is wearing the jacket she saw him in a month ago. It's wool with a fur collar that tickles her face as they hug, he also wears a pair of worn blue jeans and white sneakers. 

     

    He kisses her cheek as she pulls away and offers his cigarette to her with a playful gleam in his eye. She takes it from him and inhales deeply, grateful for the dizziness that follows and distracts her from how uncomfortable she is feeling. Zosia hasn’t said a word in greeting or made any sort of welcoming gesture, Carol can feel Zosia’s eyes on her. Carol watches as she flicks some ash off her cigarette and brings it back up to her lips, her eyes tracking both Miles and Carol’s movements. 

     

    Zosia looks amazing, as usual. She’s wearing an oversized leather bomber jacket that hangs off her shoulders in a way that makes her look soft and delicate. Underneath is a dark colored hoodie, Carol can’t tell if it’s black or blue in the low light of the street. A dark pair of jeans on her long legs and the same pair of leather boots she wore when Carol saw her a month ago. She has a pair of small gold hoops in her ears and three rings on the hand holding her cigarette, the other is buried in the pocket of her coat, hidden from Carol’s view. 

     

    “Welcome to trivia night,” Miles says excitedly, no longer entertained by the silence of the two of them staring at each other and ignoring him. 

     

    “Thank you,” Carol says, trying to keep the mocking instinct out of her voice. “Do you guys do this a lot?”

     

    “Every month.” Zosia’s first words are mumbled, she looks away from the two of them like she’s angry at herself for saying anything at all. 

     

    “Seriously?” Carol asks, unable to hide her surprise. “You guys are into bar trivia?” The mocking is no longer hidden. 

     

    “Excuse me,” Miles says, offended, he steps closer to Zosia who brings her hidden hand out of her pocket and wraps it around Miles arm. “You are looking at the 6 time champions of Dorothy’s trivia night; please treat us with the respect our titles have earned us.” He pressed a hand into his chest dramatically. “And know that if you are a weak link we will drop you from the team like that.” He snaps his fingers with a teasing smile, Zosia beside him hides a laugh as she exhales out another cloud of smoke, her eyes meeting Carol’s in amusement, before they flicker away again. 

     

    Carol raises her hands up in surrender, “I apologize, I was unaware I was in the presence of champions.” She takes another drag of Miles’ cigarette before he reaches out and takes it back from her. “I will do my best to maintain your legacy streak.” 





    Trivia is much more fun than Carol had expected it to be. She remembers Helen trying to get her to go a few times over the years, saying that they would clean up but Carol always refused. She didn’t want to be the kind of person who went to bar trivia and thought that was an appropriate way to spend a Friday night. 

     

    Sitting here at a table with Miles and Zosia as the three of them argue over answers and wait on the edge of their seats for the next question she regrets never going with Helen. Carol can see it so clearly in her mind how Helen’s competitive edge would have come out and Helen would have been calling out insults to other teams and standing up cheering, and high fiving Carol too hard in excitement, she would brag to everyone they knew about their wins and Carol would pretend she was embarrassed by it.

     

    There’s a dull ache in her chest whenever Carol thinks about all the things she missed out on experiencing with Helen because of her fear. She looks around the room at all the faces and sees unbridled joy at every table, even the losing teams are having so much fun, chatting and laughing with their friends around them. This is the world Carol had spent so long hiding from. She had hidden from the bad parts, the scary parts, the parts that had hurt her for so long. But she had also hidden from the good, from late nights out with friends and inside jokes. 

     

    She says a silent apology to Helen in her head and hopes one day she will be forgiven.

     

    Carol is relieved that she isn't holding back the team, they are in the lead so far and have been all night. Honestly, she isn’t really holding them back because she isn’t really needed, neither is Miles for that matter. Zosia has answered almost every question within seconds of it being asked. Carol had looked over to Miles after the first few questions, wondering if this was normal and he had taken an amused sip of his beer and raised his eyebrows. 

     

    Carol watched Zosia hang on every word of the questions as they were asked and she watched as the gears turned in Zosia’s head as she searched for the answer, the moment she found it her face would light up in excitement and the pencil would move in a flurry of strokes across their paper. Zosia would lean back in her chair in satisfaction, take a sip of her drink, a water, and explain the answer to Carol and Miles with the same eagerness Carol remembers her explaining the origins of the word vodka. 

     

    Zosia isn’t rude to her throughout the night but she doesn’t really speak directly to Carol either. It is very clear Zosia is happy Miles is there to act as a buffer between them and Carol feels the same. It’s nice to be in Zosia’s presence without the only feeling being some version of overwhelming guilt or sadness. With Miles there they get to laugh and sneak little glances when the other isn’t watching. The air between them feels lighter, it feels good to be around Zosia like this. 

     

    Without the pressure or the words left unsaid. The tension lingers in the quiet moments but it’s easily brushed away, and Carol isn’t pushing for more. The nerves she felt before her arrival, the need to find the perfect outfit, the torture she had put Manny through, feels silly now. Nothing was going to happen between her and Zosia tonight, she knew that and she was okay with it. Carol didn’t want anything to happen between them. She wouldn’t know what that meant, she wouldn’t know how to handle that.

     

    As the night goes on Carol starts to truly realize how much of her relationship with Zosia she missed. She had spent half of every interaction with the woman scared of the people around them and the other half trying to distract Zosia from said fear. 

     

    Watching Zosia’s face light up as she answers stupid questions about what the longest streets in Chicago are feels more intimate than a kiss. They laugh together and they celebrate together. Zosia gives Carol a high five; Zosia doesn’t even seem to register it at first. Carol answers her first question of the night, something about fireworks that Carol remembers reading off packaging years ago, and Zosia’s face splits into a wide smile and she raises a hand to Carol expectantly. The moment their hands touch Zosia realizes what she has done and she pulls her back and buries her face in the paper in front of her, writing down Carol’s answer dutifully. In the low light of the bar Carol can’t fully tell but she thinks Zosia may be blushing and she thinks that can sustain her through a whole year of waiting for Zosia. 

     

    When trivia is over they stay for another drink that Zosia gets up to grab, leaving Carol and Miles alone at the table. Carol can’t help but watch her leave as she walks away and Miles waves a hand in front of her face to get her attention. She punches him in the arm a little too hard in retaliation, it feels like no time at all has passed between them. 

     

    “You look good,” Carol says to him with a gentle smile. 

     

    He smiles happily, a hand coming to rest on the back of Carol’s chair, “So do you babe.” Carol brushes him off, her fingers running through the condensation on her empty glass.

     

    “She told you about…” Carol twirls her finger in the air in the direction Zosia walked.

     

    “Mmhmmm,” Miles hums. “Sounded like a lot.” Carol barks out a laugh.

     

    “Yeah, to say the least.” She raises her glass before she remembers it’s empty and sets it back down awkwardly. 

     

    “Are you doing okay?” Miles asks, his eyes appraising her. “Do you have someone to talk to about all this?” Carol wonders what she did to earn this, this care from Miles. This care from both of them to even invite her tonight.

     

    “Yeah,” Carol says, still overwhelmed, she sighs out the word. “My friend Manousos.” Zosia sets down a glass in front of Carol as the name leaves her lips. Her eyes staring into Carol’s with an edge. 

     

    “That asshole is still around?” Zosia asks as she takes her seat. We love him the same as we love you. A wide smile breaks out across Carol’s face.

     

    “Yeah,” she nods, her eyes shining. “That asshole is still around.”





    When they finish their next round and settle up their tab they head back outside the bar where Miles and Zosia pull out their cigarettes and light up. Miles holds his pack out to Carol who takes one and waits as he reaches into his pocket for his lighter. Before he can pull it out, Zosia steps into Carol’s space. 

     

    Her hand cups around the end of Carol’s cigarette, inches away from Carol’s mouth. Zosia’s eyes stare down where the filter disappears between Carol’s lips and she pushes down on the wheel, a flame erupting between them. Carol inhales both in surprise and to make sure the end is fully lit. 

     

    Carol stares up at Zosia, who slowly drops the hand holding the lighter down, the other coming up to grab the cigarette out from between her own lips, Zosia runs her thumb against her bottom lip, the plump flesh stretching with the touch. Carol feels it as if it’s her own mouth Zosia is touching and takes a deep breath in, trying to calm herself down. She can feel a heat blooming in her stomach as Zosia finally takes away from her and Carol forces herself not to follow. 

     

    Carol looks away needing a minute and catches Miles' eye, who just watches with raised eyebrows as the two of them awkwardly step back from one another. “Jesus,” Carol hears him mutter under his breath.

     

    “Nie rób,” Don’t, Zosia says to him. 

     

    “Czy powiedziałam coś nie tak?” Did I say something? Miles responds. 

     

    Carol’s eyes widened in surprise, “Since when do you speak Polish?”

     

    Miles smiled smugly, “I learned a few years ago; now my dear Zosia can’t hide a thing from me.” He blows smoke towards Zosia and she rolls her eyes in response, batting it away. He winks at Zosia as she turns away from both of them, Carol thinks it’s to hide a smile but she can’t be sure. 

     

    “Well my darlings,” Miles says, stretching his arms over his head, filter caught between his teeth. “I think I’m going to call it a night.” 

     

    “Do you want to share a car?” Carol asks eagerly, scared of what will happen if he leaves.

     

    “I’m just two blocks that way,” he points over Carol’s shoulder. He uses the outstretched hand to pull Carol into a hug that she sinks into. The fur of his jacket once again tickled her face. “Don’t let her scare you,” he whispers into Carol’s ear. “She’s all bark.”

     

    He turns to Zosia and pulls her into a hug as well, she goes less willingly than Carol so he lifts Zosia into the air until she starts laughing and her arms come back up around him. “Kocham cię,” I love you, Carol recognizes those words. “Jestem z ciebie dumny,” I’m proud of you, she doesn’t recognize those ones. He presses a finger into the tip of Zosia’s nose and starts walking away before Zosia can smack his hand away. 

     

    “Ja też cię kocham!” I love you too! Zosia calls after him, annoyed and fond. 

     

    They stand in silence for a moment. “Do you want to share a car?” Carol asks nervously.





    The car is silent as it inches closer and closer to Carol’s apartment. Zosia hadn’t added her own address to the ride and Carol was trying not to read into the action and failing. They both got out of the car outside of Carol’s building, Carol’s heart pounding in her chest as she walked over to the front gate. She could hear Zosia’s boots on the pavement behind her. 

     

    Carol turned back a step away from the wrought iron fencing to face Zosia. “Thank you for inviting me tonight.” Zosia nodded, her lips pressed together. “It was…” Carol smiles and looks away, embarrassed. “Really fun.” 

     

    “Yes,” Zosia says, still nodding. “I’m very good at trivia.” God, Carol loves her. 

     

    “Yes,” Carol says smiling, not bothering to hide the love in her eyes. “You are.” Carol watches as Zosia’s eyes run over her face in surprise. She looks unsettled, off balance, and Carol wonders if it was really so rare that she complimented her. Zosia looks away from Carol and pushes her hands into her pockets, her eyes darting up to Carol and away a few times before she takes a tentative step forward. 

     

    Carol holds her breath, trying not to spook her, Zosia steps even closer. Her jacket brushing against Carol's, she leans her head down and for a moment Carol thinks maybe she’s going to kiss her but instead Zosia just presses her temple into Carol’s for barely a second and then steps away. Carol can see Zosia’s throat bobbing. “Goodnight,” Zosia says, her voice heavy around the word. 

     

    “Goodnight,” Carol says back softly, watching as Zosia starts walking away, she pushes the gate open a few inches and is about to step inside.

     

    “We go every week at 7.” Zosia calls out, Carol turns back to face her immediately. “If you want to go again.” Zosia shrugs like it's nothing but Carol can see and hear the anxiety in her voice and body. 

     

    “I’ll be there,” Carol calls back, her eyes lighting up her whole face. Zosia stares at her and nods for a moment, we’ll see, her eyes say before she turns her back to Carol and disappears down the street. 

     

    Carol lays in bed that night giddy as she talks to Manousos on the phone. She had indeed woken him up but he only complained for a few minutes before getting her to tell him everything. Carol falls asleep that night and for the first time since Helen died she doesn’t feel alone. 






    2029, 4 years, 10 months, 3 weeks, and 17 days since the Unjoining.

     

    It’s been two months since Carol first went to trivia with Miles and Zosia, they have gone back every week since and won all but twice. 

     

    The first few times were more awkward between her and Zosia, they were almost scared to really speak to one another. In those first weeks Carol was so grateful for Miles presence, if there was a moment of silence or tension he found a way to defuse it with ease. Pulling Zosia into a conversation about her day or her students or the current trivia question, letting Carol collect herself as they spoke. 

     

    Zosia still doesn’t really talk directly to Carol, not much anyway, she’ll celebrate or congratulate Carol if she answers a question correctly and she will greet her and she won’t actively ignore her but she doesn’t speak to her like she does Miles. Carol doesn’t really speak to Zosia either, she’ll work up the nerve to ask Zosia something, turn towards her and then forget everything she was about to say when their eyes meet. 

     

    Carol feels crazed around Zosia, every moment she feels on the edge of being out of breath or falling out of her chair. She feels so completely powerless and unmoored around Zosia. Carol wants to be angry about it but she’s sure this is how Zosia had felt in Berlin. Scared of pushing her away or asking something she didn’t like and Carol shutting her out forever. She understands that Zosia needs this, needs to feel like she is in control of whatever is happening between them but it doesn’t mean Carol has to like it. 

     

    And it doesn't mean that Carol isn’t confused when every time after they part for the night, Zosia shares a car with Carol home, presses her face into the side of Carol’s head and then walks away, a soft, “Goodnight,” carried on the breeze back to Carol. 

     

    Even though they don’t speak beyond greetings and confusing moments of tension that Carol lies awake at night replaying in her head; Carol has learned more about Zosia in the last two months than she has known in 25 years. 

     

    They get to the bar early every night, Zosia and Miles smoke a cigarette and talk about their days, Miles will ask about Carol’s and then they will have a drink and keep talking before trivia starts.

     

    Carol gets to hear all about Zosia’s life in that hour and she treasures it every week. She gets to hear about Zosia’s classes and her students. She gets to hear Zosia tell stories about her students falling asleep or sending her disgusting emails about how sick they are. Carol gets to hear Zosia talk about how she’s concerned about a few of them who are getting lost in their research and she isn’t sure how to help them refocus. She gets to hear Zosia complain about the men in her department and the way they are coddled by the institution. 

     

    Carol learns that Zosia is tenured and that Miles is up for review. Carol gets to hear Zosia talk about her students with such care and concern and genuine affection. She thinks about what her college life would be if she had Zosia for a professor and she has a whole new level of sympathy for the girls she saw crowded and blushing around Zosia. 

     

    She learns that Zosia’s mother calls her once a week, always trying to get Zosia to visit. From what Carol has been able to gleam, Zosia’s mother still lives in Poland and Zosia refuses to go back, Carol isn’t sure why. She thinks Zosia’s father is dead because he’s never brought up and the times he is alluded to Zosia’s voice gets both dark and sad and Miles will not so subtly press a hand into Zosia’s back or shoulder or leg. 

     

    Carol realizes that she never gave much thought to Zosia’s life. What are her parents like? Did she have friends as a kid? What was it like to be gay in Poland? Zosia had said something about her shame when they spoke in her office, what shame? How much of her life had Carol missed, how much of her had Carol missed just because she was too scared to ask?

     

    Carol spends two months just listening.

     

    Zosia doesn’t drink, Zosia holds a pencil between her fingers with a white knuckle grip, Zosia doesn’t let Miles or Carol go up to the bar, she always goes for them, Zosia’s downstairs neighbor is fighting with her boyfriend and keeping her up at night. Zosia’s mother calls her one night while they are at the bar outside smoking and Carol watches her pace along the sidewalk, her shoulders hunching over, mouth wrapping around an unfamiliar language, and her face slowly dropping. 

     

    She watches as Zosia comes back and be quiet for the rest of the night, lost in her own head. Carol learns that Zosia had only been in Berlin for two months before they had met, for some reason she had thought Zosia’d been there longer. She learns Zosia was traveling all around Europe before going to grad school, that she had just finished her undergraduate degree and wanted to see all the places she had written her thesis on. 

     

    She watches Miles and Zosia together, they speak to each other with their eyes the way she would with Helen. With so much time and intimacy between them they don’t need words anymore. They fight like siblings and make up just as fast. They’re affectionate with one another and gentle with one another. Carol will watch Zosia fall into Miles as she laughs and Miles wrap his arms around her to hold her upright. Carol will watch Zosia bring a gentle hand up to his face and give him a tender smile when he talks about a boy who broke his heart—which seems to happen every other week. 

     

    She misses Helen when she watches the two of them, at first she had thought she was jealous but she isn’t angry when she sees them. She just feels sad. When she looks at them Carol sees a version of what she had and she feels sad thinking about all she missed out on during the twenty years she took for granted.





    When the weather starts warming up, Miles won’t stop talking about the beach and how excited he is to be outside again. He asks Carol if she has been to the beach yet and she laughs in his face, “Of course not,” she scoffs.

     

    Miles had gasped and said it was unacceptable, he swiftly decided for all three of them that they would be going to the beach next week and sent them both a time and place to meet for lunch. 

     

    And so Carol had started seeing them twice a week, every Friday for trivia and every Wednesday for lunch by the water; it reminded her of the residency and eating by the river. Carol wanted to say that the first time they went but she could feel that they were all thinking it and that it wouldn’t do any good to bring it up, so she didn’t.
