    “Zosia had a lot of thoughts about the books. Her favorite was the first, she thought your writing was wonderful. She always knew you were a talented writer but she was happy to finally see the proof.” Carol’s mouth skews up to one side, trying to hold herself together. She looks away, out the window to the left of the table, taking a moment, grateful that Larry doesn’t try to continue.

     

    “And did she…” Carol isn’t sure what she’s trying to ask. She can’t quite think. “And was she…” She trails off again. She takes a sharp breath in and turns back to Larry. “Was she angry about them?” 

     

    “No,” Larry says, shaking his head, incredulous. “Not really. She was grateful for them.” Carol's eyes narrow in confusion, grateful? Larry continues, “She found the books… healing but also confusing, after you left she started to doubt what the two of you had. It was very…” He trails off, into silence. “Painful, for her.” He settles one eventually. “She found great comfort in the way you wrote Lucasia and Raban.”

     

    Carol brings a hand up over her mouth, unable to speak, unable to look at him. “Out.” She says, as she turns her head to the window once more. She hears Larry get up, push in the chair, walk down the hall, and out the door. Carol stays sitting there for a while longer.

    They talk about… well…





    “How present is she?” Carol asks, her arm is around Zosia, her fingers tracing shapes into the soft skin of Zosia’s shoulder. Her stomach turns as she asks the question, she refuses to look over at Zosia even as she feels the younger woman turn her head looking up at Carol.

Carol is wearing a brown knit sweater and blue jeans with bare feet. Zosia is wearing one of Carol’s flannel shirts over a sundress and leaning back against Carol with her legs curled up in front of her.

    They go out to a gallery opening for one of Miles' friends and Carol feels something buzzing in her veins the whole evening. She hasn’t been to a gallery show in decades, probably since Berlin. The closest she had come was wandering the Georgia O’Keefe museum during the Joining. She knocks the memories of that time out of her head as Miles hands her a plastic cup full of water. Carol takes a sip of it as her eyes roam around the room.

     

    Carol had tried to dress “cool” knowing the type of people that Miles is friends with and the type of people that are likely to be filling the gallery. She also wanted to look hot, she wanted to feel Zosia’s eyes on her. The weather was warmer so Carol forwent a jacket and wore a tight white t-shirt with short sleeves that showed off her toned arms. The frustration of the last year has led Carol to have a much more active exercise routine to work off said… frustrations. 

     

    The shirt is tucked into a pair of high waisted, pleated, black pants and on Carol's feet are a pair of leather boots with a once inch heel. Carol recently got her hair cut, it had grown down past her shoulders and she was getting tired of how much maintenance it took. She would do this every few years, let her hair grow long and then cut it short again, she had gotten her hair cut just before her and Helen set off on Carol’s last book tour. She got it cut to the same length, soft blonde waves with the ends just brushing the bottom of her jawline, it felt good to have one less thing to worry about when she woke up in the morning. 

     

    Carol worried Zosia wouldn’t like it, or that it would be triggering? She hadn’t had her hair this sort since the Joining and Carol was worried it would bring back too many memories between them. 

     

    Miles loved the haircut, he had immediately started trying to touch it and Carol had slapped his hands away playfully before letting him brush his hands along the bottom edges and let out a low, “Ooohhh,” at the softness of her hair while Carol laughed at how ridiculous he was.

     

    Her and Miles had arrived late since they had shared a car, Zosia was coming from work so she arrived alone. Zosia had texted that she was there but they hadn’t seen her yet. Carol’s eyes roamed around the room looking for her and Miles’ eyes roamed around the room looking for people he knew. 

     

    When Carol finally spots her, Zosia talking to someone Carol doesn’t recognise but Miles clearly does, a smile spreads across his face and he pulls Carol across the room towards Zosia and the stranger. 

     

    As they approach Carol can see Zosia through the crowd more clearly, her eyes drag slowly up Zosia’s form.

     

    On Zosia’s feet are a pair of white Samba’s with black stripes and a pair of red socks that hit her mid-calf and shorts. They go down to below Zosia’s knees and they’re loose, they’re the kind of shorts Carol has seen skateboarding teenagers wear. They’re oversized, mauve cargo shorts made out of some nylon fabric. Zosia’s calves flex as she shifts on her feet from side to side, her hands slipping into the shorts pockets.

    She’s wearing a tight, black, long sleeve shirt that looks painted onto her forearms and a matching oversized black t-shirt on top of it. Zosia’s chain sits on top of the fabric and Carol fights the desire storm across the room, wrap her hand around it, and pull Zosia in closer. 

     

    Zosia has also been letting her hair grow out, it's the longest Carol has ever seen it before. It falls about an inch, maybe two, below Zosia’s shoulders in waves that perfectly frame Zosia’s face. The thick dark waves make Zosia’s eyes look more intense and her cheekbones even sharper. 

     

    Zosia’s dressed like a teenage skater boy and Carol thinks she might go insane before she and Miles even reach her.

Carol watches with wide eyes, stepping away from the two of them closer to Zosia, who she sees is very subtly trying to check Carol out. 

     

    Carol crosses her arms over her chest, both to hide her preening and to make her arms more visible. She’s watching Zosia’s eyes run over her when Miles interrupts them. “Carol,” he says, his voice admonishing as he looks from her to Zosia. “This is Melissa, the artist.” Carol’s head tilts back in understanding and she holds a hand out, which Melissa takes with a kind smile. 

     

    “It’s great to meet you, Carol.” She has an accent that Carol can’t place when she speaks, “Miles is always speaking very fondly of you.” Melissa is about Carol's age, she has blonde hair that is pushed back on her head and probably hits between her shoulder blades. Her eyes are a light blue and she’s wearing a white button down that Carol can tell from the way it sits is very expensive.

     

    “It's nice to meet you too, Miles was singing your praises the whole way here.” Carol raises her eyebrows teasing Miles and he scoffs, pushing at her shoulder playfully. “How do you all know each other?” Carol asks, looking between the three of them. Miles and Zosia’s eyes jump to each other and Carol, along with Melissa, watches them in confusion at their sudden tension.

Did the two of them think this wouldn’t come up? How stupid are they? Carol rolls her eyes internally at them. 

     

    Carol knows Zosia hasn’t been celibate since Berlin for god's sake. Every other week at trivia someone approaches their table acting way too familiar with Zosia for Carol’s taste. Carol will just glare at the table until they are politely sent on their way and Miles changes the subject.

Zosia beside her is frozen in some horrified expression like she just sucked on a lemon, Miles is looking at Carol like she’s a bomb about to go off. But Carol looks at Melissa in front of her and she just wants to laugh. 

     

    Zoë had told Carol that Zosia had a type and Carol had seen the girls at the bar but Melissa in front of her is all but Carol’s twin. An amused smile spreads across Carol’s face as she turns to look at Zosia like, really?

     

    Zosia avoids her eye taking a long pull from her cup of water. Miles lets out a relieved breath, and Melissa watches feeling like she is missing something huge. 

     

    “Really?” Carol asks eagerly, turning to face Melissa. “What happened there?” 

     

    “Oh, nothing exciting,” Melissa says with a wave of her hand. “She was clearly hung up on her ex and I don’t like competing with ghosts.” She winks at Zosia as she speaks and Zosia’s eyes slip closed and Carol can practically see her wishing the ground would swallow her whole.

     

    Does the whole city know that Zosia never got over her?

     

    Carol tries and fails to stifle her smile as she looks over at Zosia, she's such an idiot, Carol wants to run her tongue over the side of Zosia’s face, press her body as tight as she can against Zosia’s, wrap a leg around her waist, and just breathe.

     

    “That’s too bad,” Carol says as she turns back to Melissa, whose eyes are looking between Carol and Zosia, the pieces coming together in her mind.

     

    A slow smile spreads across Melissa’s face, “It all works out in the end.” She tucks an arm around Miles’ and turns to him, “Come on, darling, I’ll give you the tour.” She gives Carol a conspiratorial smile and they disappear into the crowd behind them, leaving Carol and Zosia behind. 

     

    Carol takes a step forward and turns around to face Zosia with a teasing smile, “She seemed nice.”

     

    “Yes,” Zosia eyes her wearily. 

     

    “Very blonde,” Carol jokes, her eyebrows scrunching together as she nods. 

     

    Zosia groans and takes a step closer to Carol, “Can we just ignore it?” Her eyes pleading, “Please?” Zosia bends her knees slightly, bringing them to eye level. Carol thinks about drawing it out, all the times Zosia has teased her for her books running through her head but she decides to take the high road. And she likes the begging look in Zosia’s eye too much to deny her. 

     

    “Fine,” Carol says with a roll of her eyes. “But go get me some food before I change my mind.” Zosia rises back up to her full height and smiles widely, Carol will never get used to being on the receiving end of Zosia’s smile. She doesn’t understand how everyone in the room doesn’t stop what they are doing when Zosia smiles just to stare at it. 

     

    “Yes ma’am,” Zosia says cheekily before she sets off. Carol watches her go, her eyes roaming over Zosia’s body as she moves and her skin tingles. 





    They’ve been at the gallery for just under two hours now. Carol and Zosia are walking around the room lazily, taking in the painting on the gallery walls at a leisurely pace. They move in sync around one another, their bodies naturally falling forward or back in perfect time to let the other get closer or back away from the work. 

     

    When they’ve been around the whole room twice they find the only area of the room that isn’t crowded, which happens to be the dead center, and they start talking. Carol is telling Zosia about a phone call she had with Val and Zosia is standing just inches away with her arms crossed over her chest, her head angled down to look at Carol and a hint of a smile curling the corners of her mouth up. Her eyes trace over Carol's lips as she speaks, using the excuse that it’s loud in the room to get away with the intensity of her gaze. Carol keeps talking even though her heart is pounding in her chest from how close Zosia is standing and the way she is staring.

     

    There are trays going around the room with little deserts as the night winds down. A waiter stops beside them with one of the trays full of chocolate iced mini cupcakes and Carol is about to wave him away when Zosia’s face lights up and she grabs one. Zosia mumbles out a thank you to the server, her mouth already full as she shoves the whole in.. 

     

    Carol watches her eat with equal parts disgust and affection. 

     

    Without a thought or a moment of concern about who is in the room or who might see; Carol reaches up and wipes off some chocolate icing from Zosia’s lip with her thumb. Carol barely even realizes what she is doing as she moves. The feeling of Zosia’s lip under her finger sparks down her wrist and Carol pulls her hand back like she has been burned. 

     

    She looks down at her thumb, covered in chocolate icing, it feels as though she has been electrocuted where the two of them touched. Carol brings her thumb up to her own mouth, her lips wrap around the tip of her finger and she sucks off the icing trying to ease the feeling that has built under her skin. 

     

    She releases her thumb when the tingling fades into a low hum. Her eyes look back up to Zosia’s and she finds Zosia’s eyes piercing into her. Zosia’s lips are parted and her chest is rising with a heavy breath as she stares down at Carol.

     

    The two of them are closer than Carol remembers and getting even closer still. Carol’s eyes move back up to Zosia’s and she watches as Zosia’s hand comes up to the side of Carol’s head. The world moves slowly around them as Zosia pulls Carol in at the same time Carol steps closer. 

     

    Zosia’s forehead presses into Carol’s and her nose presses into the skin of Carol’s cheek. Her other hand wraps around Carol’s waist to hold her close and one of Carol hands settles on Zosia’s hip under her shirt, one of her fingers brushes against bare skin and she feels Zosia shiver against her. Carol’s right hand comes up to hold Zosia’s face against hers. Zosia presses in harder against her, their heads grinding together at the force. Carols can feel Zosia’s breath against her lips and her jaw drops open at the feeling. 

     

    “I love you,” Zosia says, in a desperate whimper, her hand on the back of Carol's head tangling in her hair and holding Carol firmly against her, like she’s scared Carol will move away. 

     

    Carol’s breath stutters in her chest at the sound of the words and she feels relief floods her, her body sags forward into Zosia knowing the taller woman will catch her. Carol shifts her hand over from the side of Zosia’s face, her thumb runs over Zosia’s bottom lip. Carol tilts her head back to watch her thumb move against Zosia and she presses the pad of her finger against Zosia’s mouth and watches the plump flesh shift around her touch and she feels the warmth of Zosia’s mouth against her finger and she presses harder.

     

    She looks back up into Zosia’s eyes, “I love you too.” Zosia’s eyes slip closed and a whisper of a smile forms into one blinding happiness as her eyes open. Carol smiles back at her, as widely as she ever has in her life. Carol laughs against Zosia, feeling Zosia’s hands tighten even further around her.

     

    Zosia surges forward, closing the half inch of space between their mouths and wraps her lips around Carol’s. Carol gasps against her and falls forward in surprise. Her mouth falls open against Zosia's and she presses impossibly closer. Their lips part for barely even a second to groan into each other, Zosia’s hand pulls as Carol’s hair making her legs go weak and Carol’s hand holds Zosia’s face so tightly she can feel Zosia whining against her and kissing her deeper.  Their tongues meet and Carol moans into Zosia’s mouth and Zosia’s arm wraps around Carol’s waist, they’re faces are pressed together so closely that Carol can’t move at all, their lips meet and part and meet and part and Carol can taste salt on her tongue and she isn’t sure which one of them is crying but she doesn't care. She moans into Zosia’s mouth and her nails dig into the skin on Zosia’s hip until the younger woman hisses against her and Carol swallows down the noise. 

     

    The world around them keeps turning, the guests keep talking, a few people look at them in surprise but no one says anything and Carol doesn't care at all. She doesn't even notice. It doesn't even register to her where they are or that people can see them. All she can think or feel or hear or taste is Zosia and how she never wants to be apart from her again.

     

    Zosia pulls her head away and Carol chases her, breathless. Zosia tilts her head forward, keeping Carol at bay. Her lips are red from where Carol has sucked and bit at them and she feels crazed at the sight of them. “We’re in public,” Zosia says, gasping for air around the words, her eyes darting around the room before returning to Carol, who hasn’t stopped looking at Zosia. 

     

    “So what?” Carol mumbles, her thumb brushing over Zosia’s cheekbone reverently.

     

    Mine.

     

    Yours.

    Chapter 19: Epilogue: Forgiveness

    Chapter Notes

    I can't believe it's over :'(

    This is the longest piece of writing I have ever written and it has been so incredible to see so many people connecting with it.

    Thank you all for reading and for commenting and for tweeting and for your kudos <3 enjoy :)

    2030, 5 years, 10 months, 4 weeks, and 3 days since the Unjoining.

     

    They leave the gallery and walk down the street hand in hand.

     

    Carol isn’t really sure where they are going and she doesn’t really care, she’s to busy staring down at her hand in Zosia’s. She squeezes Zosia’s hand like you would pinch yourself to check if you were dreaming. She remembers the feeling of Zosia’s hand in hers from Berlin but she remembers the feeling of pulling her hand away even more. She remembers how she would slip her hand out of Zosia’s and the taller woman would turn her head away to take a drag of her cigarette or she would send Carol a teasing smile with tight eyes and Carol would blush and cross her arms over her chest both in embarrassment and to keep her hands out of reach.

     

    Carol runs her thumb over the back of Zosia’s hand and she marvels at the soft skin she finds there and she once again thinks about all the things she has denied herself in favor of a life that didn’t exist, a life half lived. She thinks about Helen and how she would press her hand into Carol’s back when they were out on the street but her hand wouldn’t linger for longer than a second. How they would lean their heads toward each other when they wanted to kiss but their lips would never touch. Carol runs her thumb against Zosia’s hand and she stares down at their linked fingers in wonder at how easy it feels, how easy it could have always felt.

     

    Zosia turns at the feeling, her eyes locking with Carol’s as a warm smiles spreads across her face. Carol pulls them to a stop, her hand tightening around Zosia’s, her free hand comes up to the side of Zosia’s head she pulls her down. She presses their foreheads together and she watches Zosia’s eyes slip closed in surrender, Carol brings their lips together and sighs in relief. She can feel the smile on Zosia’s face against mouth and she can’t believe this is real.

     

    Zosia takes a step away after a moment, breaking their kiss and she pulls Carol along behind her. “Come on,” Zosia says softly and Carol’s slow, dreamlike steps follow her without complaint.

     

    The streets are still unfamiliar to Carol so she stays focus on the woman beside her. She pulls Zoisa’s hand up and over her head to wrap around her shoulders. She keeps their fingers linked and Carol’s hand lays limply against her chest as Zosia’s thumb rubs over her fingertips and she pulls Carol tighter against her, tucking Carol into her armpit as they walk. It’s hard to move with Zosia pressed so close but neither of them complain and neither of them try to move away, they just slow their pace and savor the feeling.

     

    They talk about Melissa’s art and they talk about their days and they laugh as they walk and Carol turns her head to press it into Zosia’s neck to quiet herself and Zosia’s tucks her head down over Carol’s and a warmth spreads through Carol’s chest and her hand squeezes Zosia’s against her shoulder.

     

    They stop at a red light and Carol stares over at their linked fingers against her shoulder, she feels a tremor run down her arm from where they touch. She feels Zosia press her face into the side of her head and inhale deeply. She feels Zosia press her lips into her hair. Carol turns to face Zosia and she lets her eyes linger on her lips and her head lean back, Zosia leans in and brings their mouths back together in a slow kiss, her lips dragging over Carol’s in long strokes and her arm wraps tighter around Carol who opens her mouth wider to draw Zosia’s tongue inside and is rewarded with the slow slide of Zosia’s against her own.

     

    Carol hears people walk around them on the corner and she knows the light has changed to green but she doesn’t care and neither does Zosia.

     

     

     

     

    They walk 6 more blocks their hangs swinging between them, Zosia’s other hand holds a cigarette which she occasionally passes over to Carol. Zosia leads them right by the turn that would bring them to Carol’s apartment and Carol turns, confused, to Zosia. “Where are we going?” She asks, looking back over her shoulder down her street.

     

    “My place,” Zosia says as she exhales and glances over at Carol with a pleased smile.

     

    “Oh,” Carol breathes out.

     

    She’s never seen Zosia’s apartment. She does’t even know where Zosia lives really. She has heard Zosia talk about her place. She’s heard Zosia talk about how her neighbors were driving her crazy with their fighting but that was months ago and Carol thinks they moved out; or broke up.

     

    They walk 3 more blocks before Zosia stops suddenly and their hands stretch between them but never break as Carol stops a few paces away. She turns back in confusion while Zosia pulls a set of keys out of her pocket and tugs Carol back toward the front gate.

     

    Carol eyebrows furrow in confusion and she looks over Zosia’s shoulder, back down the way they came, she can see the corner of her building from here. Her hand drops out of Zosia’s in shock and because Zosia lets it go without a fight to push open the gate. She stands with her back against it as Carol looks back and forth between Zosia’s building and hers.

     

    “This is where you live?” Carol asks—stunned, a finger pointing over at the building beside them. It’s a two story orange brick building with a ground floor entrance. Above the front door on the second story of the building is a window and through it Carol can see a staircase and a simple light hanging from the ceiling.

     

    “Yes,” Zosia says simply, she takes another drag in, clearly enjoying Carols’ reaction. “I’ve lived here four years, you’re the one following me, kochanie.”

     

    Zosia says the word with so much tenderness and with such a smug smile that it's all Carol can do not to fall to her knees right there on the street and crawl to her, to press her face into Zosia’s thigh and beg her to never leave.

     

    She walks through the gate Zosia holds open and cups her hand around Zosia’s jaw as she passes and feels Zosia nuzzle into it before her fingertips slip away.

     

    The walk up to Zosia’s apartment is quick just a single flight of stairs. Outside the door is a surprisingly large landing with a shoe rack full of boots, sneakers, loafers, cleats and a few pairs of sandals. Beside the shoe rack is a wire basket with umbrellas and what looks like a bicycle pump. Does Zosia bike?

     

     

     

     

    In a month, Zosia will arrive to the bar late for trivia. Carol and Miles will be smoke outside waiting for her. She will pull up on a bike wearing a pair of oversized black cargo shorts and mid-calf high socks and sneakers. She’ll have a skintight, white t-shirt on and her gold chain will be rising and falling against her chest as Zosia pants; out of breath from how hard she was peddling.

     

    Her arms and neck will be coated in a thin layer of sweat and Miles will complain about how she smells and Carol will try and fail to hold herself back from licking a taste off of Zosia’s skin. Miles will through his cigarette down to the ground and storm inside while calling back to the two of them that he hates them and they won’t hear him because Zosia will have Carol pressed into the wall of the building and Carol’s hands will be resting just below Zosia’s collarbones holding the weight of her breath in her hands.

     

     

     

     

    Zosia walks by Carol toward the shoe rack and she slips her shoes off, she tucks them into an empty space and Carol follows suit. She pulls off her boots and sets them neatly on the ground beside the umbrellas. Outside the front door is a black welcome matte with a white lettering that reads, “Witamy,” and then underneath it is, “(Welcome).” Carol stares at it with a bemused smile and looks up to Zosia who blushes.

     

    “Miles bought it for me when I moved in,” she grumbles, a key slips into the lock.

     

    “Of course,” Carol says with a huffed laugh.

     

    “He came over before I had put it out and spent the whole night making little comments until I pulled it out of the closet… its been there ever since.” Zosia explains with a fond roll of her eyes as the door swings open and Carol takes her first steps into Zosia’s apartment.

     

    It’s dark, the only light is streaming in from beyond the linen curtains hung over Zosia’s windows. Carol hears the door close behind her and the sound of a light switch being flicked. Lamps come on around the apartment and Carol’s eyes roam over everything.

     

    The apartment is one big room, its probably about the size of Carol’s living room, which is saying something because Carol’s living room is huge.

     

    To her left is Zosia’s kitchen, it’s beautiful and has clearly been remodeled. The cabinets are flat panels painted white and the handles are stainless steel ledges. The sink is a double basin farm house sink that looks like it could be original to the building from how old it is. The porcelain is thick and bubbled, crafted in a way that looks too expert to have been made in the last 50 years. The kitchen island is a large, stainless steel counter with exposed shelves below it where Carol can see pots and pans and extra towels stacked neatly beside them. On top of the island are two piles of unopened mail and shitty magazines, a bowl full of onions and garlic and another full of both regular and sweet potatoes. The fridge is covered in postcards and photos of people that Carol doesn’t recognize, all of it is held up by colorful, bubble letter magnets of the alphabet. Against the wall next to the island is a map of the Gdańsk public transit system.

    To Carol’s left is a black and white tiled bathroom with a vintage clawfoot tub; hung around it is a transparent, neon yellow shower curtain.

She’ll be standing on one leg with the other thrown over Zosia’s shoulder as her head moves between Carol’s legs. Carol can see on the frames of the closest entry where Zosia has taken off the doors and she wonders what was done with them.

Carol will pace and rant in front of Zosia’s closet and wonder if she should change or if she should put her hair up or put on mascara and Zosia will grab Carol’s frantically moving hands and hold her face in her hands and tell her that her mother will love Carol just as she is. She will press a kiss into Carol’s lips and her door will buzz a moment later and Zosia will disappear out the door to help her mother up the stairs.

After she leaves when she calls Zosia she make her daughter pass the phone to Carol to hear how she is doing because Zosia doesn’t tell her enough detail and Carol will curl up against Zosia’s chest and tell her mother about her life. Zosia’s mother will start sending them two bags of those chocolates that Carol likes and she will start sending Carol handwritten reviews of her books that both charm and weird Carol out.

     

    Carol will sometimes curl up into a ball on their bed or on their couch and will herself not to cry thinking about how easy it is for Zosia’s mother to love her and how hard it was for her own. Zosia will find her when she gets like this and hold her and turn on Golden Girls and run her fingers through Carol’s hair and whisper that she loves her.

     

     

     

     

    Along the farthest wall, looking out over the street are large windows with thin, linen curtains and under the windows is Zosia’s bed. Its a queen sized mattress, low to the ground and sitting on a wooden frame. The comforter cover is a pattern of thick, alternating, blue and white stripes. Beside the bed are two wooden nightstands, both of them have lamps on top, the one closest to Carol came on when Zosia flipped the light switch and beside it is a pile of books and a pair of reading glasses.

     

    On the floor next to the bed is another pile of books and a stack of stapled together sheets of paper with a red pen sitting on top of them.

    Across from the bed is a navy blue couch with a red, knit blanket folded on one of its cushions. Next to the couch are three stacks of books that come up almost as high as the kitchen counter. There is a window beside them and Carol can see a full ashtray on the ledge.

The whole apartment smells of Zosia, which, of course. But Carol somehow hadn’t realized how intense it would be. In Berlin it had been diluted by Zosia’s roommates, here Carol feels every beat of Zosia’s presence without interruption or distraction.
